Table 3.1
:
Comparison of semi-honest 3PC frameworks for PPML

3.1
Preliminaries and Definitions

We consider
3
parties denoted by
\mathcal{P}=\{P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}\}
that are connected by pair-wise private and authentic channels in a synchronous network, and a static, semi-honest adversary that can corrupt at most one party.

3.1.1
Sharing Semantics

For the arithmetic and boolean sharing, we follow a
(3,1)
replicated secret sharing (RSS), where a value
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
is split into three shares. Two of the shares (
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2})
can be generated in the preprocessing phase independent of the value to be shared, and their sum can be interpreted as a mask (
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
). The third share, dependent on
\mathsf{v}
, can be computed in the online phase and can be treated as the masked value
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
.

Table 3.2
:
Semantics for
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
in
ASTRA
.
†
†
nicematrix-placeholder:
NiceTabular (nicematrix)

Next, we distinguish the three parties into two sets; the
eval
set
\mathcal{E}=\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
which is assigned the task of carrying out the computation, and is active throughout the online phase. The
helper
set
\mathcal{D}=\{P_{0}\}
, is used to assist
\mathcal{E}
in preparing the preprocessing material, and so it is only active in the preprocessing phase. Complying with the roles and RSS format, the distribution is done as follows:
P_{0}:\{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}\},P_{1}:\{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}\}
, and
P_{2}:\{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}\}
.

The RSS sharing semantics is presented in Table
3.2
, denoted by
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
, along with the semantics for
\left[\cdot\right]
-sharing. Both the sharings used are linear i.e. given sharings of
\mathsf{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{v}_{m}
and public constants
c_{1},\ldots,c_{m}
, sharing of
\sum_{i=1}^{m}c_{i}\mathsf{v}_{i}
can be computed non-interactively for an integer
m
.

Notation 3.1

(a) For the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of
n
values
\mathsf{a}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{a}_{n}
,
\gamma_{\mathsf{a}_{1}\ldots\mathsf{a}_{n}}=\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}
and
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{1}\ldots\mathsf{a}_{n}}{}=\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}{}
(b) We use superscripts
{\bf B}
, and
{\bf G}
to denote sharing semantics in boolean, and garbled world, respectively–
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
,
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
. We omit the superscript for arithmetic world.

Sharing semantics for boolean sharing over
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is similar to arithmetic sharing except that addition is replaced with XOR. The semantics for garbled sharing are described in §
3.3
with the relevant context.

3.2
Arithmetic / Boolean 3PC

This section covers the details of our 3PC semi-honest protocol
ASTRA
over an arithmetic ring
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
. The protocol primarily consists of the following primitives – i) Sharing §
3.2.1
, ii) Multiplication §
3.2.2
, and iii) Reconstruction §
3.2.3
.

3.2.1
Sharing

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
(Fig.
3.1
) enables
P_{i}
to generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-share of a value
\mathsf{v}
. During the preprocessing phase,
\mathsf{\lambda}
-shares are sampled non-interactively using the pre-shared keys (cf. §
2.5.1
) in a way that
P_{i}
will get the entire mask
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
. During the online phase,
P_{i}
computes
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
and sends to
P_{1},P_{2}
.
For the special case when
P_{0}
wants to perform a
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of
\mathsf{v}
in the preprocessing, the communication can be optimized further. For this, parties set
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=0
.
P_{0},P_{1}
sample
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}
non-interactively.
P_{0}
computes and sends
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}=-(\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1})
to
P_{2}
.

Input(s):

P_{i}:\mathsf{v}
,
Output:

\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

Sample as follows:
P_{i},P_{0},P_{1}:\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}
,
P_{i},P_{0},P_{2}:\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}
.

\justify

Online:

P_{i}
computes
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
and sends to
P_{1},P_{2}
.

Figure 3.1
:
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of a value
\mathsf{v}
by party
P_{i}
in
ASTRA
.

Lemma 3.1
(Communication)

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
(Fig.
3.1
) requires a communication of at most
2\ell
bits and
1
round in the online phase.

Proof 3.2.2
.

The preprocessing of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
is non-interactive as the parties sample non interactively using key setup
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{Key}}
(§
2.5.1
). In the online phase,
P_{i}
sends
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{1},P_{2}
resulting in 1 round and communication of at most
2\ell
bits (
P_{i}=P_{0}
).

3.2.1.1
Joint Sharing

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
enables parties
P_{i},P_{j}
to generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-share of a value
\mathsf{v}
. In
ASTRA
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
is used to enable
P_{1},P_{2}
generate
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
non-interactively. For this, parties set
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}=0
and
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}
.

3.2.2
Multiplication

Given the shares of
\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b}
, the goal of the multiplication protocol is to generate shares of
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{ab}
. The protocol is designed such that parties
P_{1},P_{2}
obtain a masked version of the output
\mathsf{z}
, say
\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}
in the online phase, and
P_{0}
obtain the mask
\mathsf{r}
in the preprocessing phase. Parties then generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of these values, and locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{r}\rrbracket
to obtain the final output.

Online

Note that,

\displaystyle\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{ab}-\mathsf{r}=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}})-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\footnotesize{(cf. notation\penalty\ \ref{notation:3pcSconcise})}}

(3.1)

In Eq
3.2.2
,
P_{1},P_{2}
can compute
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}
locally, and hence we are interested in computing
\mathsf{y}=(\mathsf{z-r})-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}
. Let
\mathsf{y}=\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{y}_{2}
, where
\mathsf{y}_{1}
and
\mathsf{y}_{2}
can be computed respectively by
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
.

\displaystyle P_{1}:\mathsf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r}\right]_{1}

\displaystyle P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r}\right]_{2}

(3.2)

The preprocessing is set up such that
P_{1},P_{2}
receive an additive sharing (
\left[\cdot\right]
) of
\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r}
. Parties
P_{1},P_{2}
mutually exchange the missing share to reconstruct
\mathsf{y}
and subsequently
\mathsf{z-r}
.

\mathsf{isTr}
is a bit denoting whether truncation is required (
\mathsf{isTr}=1
) or not (
\mathsf{isTr}=0
).

Input(s):

\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
.

Output:

\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket
where
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=0
and
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{ab}
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

1.

P_{0},P_{j}
sample
{\mathsf{u}}^{j}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
. Let
{\mathsf{u}^{1}}+\mathsf{u}^{2}=\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r}
for
\mathsf{r}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

2.

Party
P_{0}
: Computes
\mathsf{r}=\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-{\mathsf{u}^{1}}-\mathsf{u}^{2}
. If
\mathsf{isTr}=1
, sets
\mathsf{q}=\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}
, else
\mathsf{q}=\mathsf{r}
.

Executes
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}(P_{0},\mathsf{q})
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket
.

\justify

Online:

Let
\mathsf{y}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}
.

1.

Compute:
P_{1}:\mathsf{y}_{1}=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+{\mathsf{u}}^{1},\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{2}=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+{\mathsf{u}}^{2}

2.

P_{1}
sends
\mathsf{y}_{1}
to
P_{2}
, while
P_{2}
sends
\mathsf{y}_{2}
to
P_{1}
, and they locally compute
\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}=\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}
.

3.

P_{1},P_{2}
: If
\mathsf{isTr}=1
, set
\mathsf{p}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})^{\mathsf{t}}
, else
\mathsf{p}=\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}
. Execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}(P_{1},P_{2},\mathsf{p})
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket
.

4.

Compute
\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket
. Here
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{z}
otherwise.

Figure 3.2
:
Multiplication with / without truncation in
ASTRA
.

Preprocessing

Parties
P_{1},P_{2}
should obtain
\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r}\right]
while
P_{0}
should obtain
\mathsf{r}
. For this,
P_{0},P_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
non-interactively sample
\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r}\right]_{i}
. This enables
P_{0}
to obtain
\mathsf{r}
in clear as it can compute
\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}
locally.

Lemma 3.2.3

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
(Fig.
3.2
) (in
ASTRA
) requires
\ell
bits of communication in the preprocessing, and
1
round and
2\ell
bits of communication in the online phase.

Proof 3.2.4
.

During preprocessing, sampling of
{\mathsf{u}}^{1},{\mathsf{u}}^{2}
are performed non-interactively using
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{Key}}
. A communication of
\ell
bits is required for the sharing of
\mathsf{q}
by
P_{0}
.
During online,
P_{1},P_{2}
exchange
\mathsf{y}_{1},\mathsf{y}_{2}
values in parallel resulting in a communication of
2\ell
bits and 1 round.

3.2.2.1
Truncation

To accommodate truncation, the multiplication protocol is modified as follows.
P_{1},P_{2}
locally truncate
(\mathsf{z-r})
and generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of it in the online phase. Similarly,
P_{0}
truncates
\mathsf{r}
in the preprocessing and generates its
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares. Parties locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket=\llbracket(\mathsf{z-r})^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket
.

3.2.2.2
Multiplication with constant

Multiplication by a constant in MPC is typically local. Given constant
\alpha
and
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
, the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of the product
\mathsf{y}=\alpha\mathsf{v}
can be locally computed as per (
3.3
).

\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}}=\alpha\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{u}},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{1}=\alpha\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{2}=\alpha\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}

(3.3)

However, in FPA, we need to perform a truncation on the output. Let
\alpha\mathsf{v}=\beta^{1}+\beta^{2}
where
\beta^{1}=\alpha.\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
and
\beta^{2}=\alpha.(-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2})
.
P_{1},P_{2}
truncate
\beta^{1}
and generate its arithmetic sharing using
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
, while
P_{0}
does the same with
\beta^{2}
.

3.2.3
Reconstruction

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}(\mathcal{P},\mathsf{v})
(Fig.
3.3
) enables parties in
\mathcal{P}
to compute
\mathsf{v}
, given its
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-share. Note that each party misses one share to reconstruct the output, and the other two parties hold this share. One out of the two parties will send the missing share to the party that lacks it. Reconstruction towards a single party can be viewed as a special case.

\justify

Input(s):

\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
,
Output:

\mathsf{v}
.

1.

P_{0}
sends
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}
to
P_{2}
;
P_{0}
sends
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}
to
P_{1}
;
P_{1}
sends
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{0}
.

2.

Compute
\mathsf{v}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}
.

Figure 3.3
:
Reconstruction of value
\mathsf{v}
among
\mathcal{P}
in
ASTRA
.

Lemma 3.2.5

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}
(Fig.
3.3
) requires a communication of
3\ell
bits and
1
round in the online phase.

3.2.4
Multi-input Multiplication

3-input multiplication

To compute
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{abc}
, note that

\displaystyle\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{abc}-\mathsf{r}=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}})-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{abc}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ac}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bc}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}-\mathsf{r}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\footnotesize{(cf. notation\penalty\ \ref{notation:3pcSconcise})}}

(3.4)

Similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
, for
\mathsf{y}=(\mathsf{z-r})-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{abc}}
, let
\mathsf{y}=\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{y}_{2}
, where
\mathsf{y}_{1}
and
\mathsf{y}_{2}
can be computed respectively by
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
.

\displaystyle P_{1}:\mathsf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bc}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ac}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}\right]_{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}\right]_{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}\right]_{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}+\mathsf{r}\right]_{1}

\displaystyle P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bc}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ac}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}\right]_{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}\right]_{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}\right]_{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}+\mathsf{r}\right]_{2}

(3.5)

To generate
\left[\mathsf{x}\right]
for
\mathsf{x}\in\{\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}},\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}},\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}\}
,
P_{0},P_{1}
non-interactively sample
P_{1}
’s share.
P_{0}
computes the share of
P_{2}
and communicates to it. The generation of
\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}+\mathsf{r}\right]
and the rest of the steps follow similar to that of 2-input multiplication protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
in §
3.2.2
. The formal protocol appears in Fig.
3.4
.

\mathsf{isTr}
is a bit denoting whether truncation is required (
\mathsf{isTr}=1
) or not (
\mathsf{isTr}=0
).

Input(s):

\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{c}\rrbracket
.

Output:

\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket
where
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=0
and
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{abc}
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

1.

For each
\mathsf{x}\in\{\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}},\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}},\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}\}
,
P_{0},P_{1}
sample
{\mathsf{x}}^{1}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.
P_{0}
computes and sends
{\mathsf{x}}^{2}={\mathsf{x}}-{\mathsf{x}}^{1}
to
P_{2}
.

2.

P_{0},P_{j}
sample
{\mathsf{u}}^{j}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
. Let
{\mathsf{u}^{1}}+\mathsf{u}^{2}=\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}+\mathsf{r}
for
\mathsf{r}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

3.

Party
P_{0}
: Computes
\mathsf{r}={\mathsf{u}^{1}}+\mathsf{u}^{2}-\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}
. If
\mathsf{isTr}=1
, sets
\mathsf{q}=\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}
, else
\mathsf{q}=\mathsf{r}
.

Executes
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}(P_{0},\mathsf{q})
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket
.

\justify

Online:

Let
\mathsf{y}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}
.

1.

Locally compute:

\displaystyle P_{1}:\mathsf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bc}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ac}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}\right]_{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}\right]_{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}\right]_{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-{\mathsf{u}}^{1},

\displaystyle P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bc}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ac}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}\right]_{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}\right]_{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}\right]_{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-{\mathsf{u}}^{2}

2.

P_{1}
sends
\mathsf{y}_{1}
to
P_{2}
, while
P_{2}
sends
\mathsf{y}_{2}
to
P_{1}
, and they locally compute
\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}=\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}
.

3.

P_{1},P_{2}
: If
\mathsf{isTr}=1
, set
\mathsf{p}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})^{\mathsf{t}}
, else
\mathsf{p}=\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}
. Execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}(P_{1},P_{2},\mathsf{p})
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket
.

4.

Compute
\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket
. Here
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{z}
otherwise.

Figure 3.4
:
Three-input Multiplication with / without truncation in
ASTRA
.

Lemma 3.2.6

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult3}}
(Fig.
3.4
) (in
ASTRA
) requires
4\ell
bits of communication in the preprocessing, and
1
round and
2\ell
bits of communication in the online phase.

Proof 3.2.7
.

During preprocessing,
\ell
bits of communication from
P_{0}
to
P_{2}
is required to generate
\left[\cdot\right]
-shares of each of
\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}},\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}
, and
\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}
. The sampling of
{\mathsf{u}}^{1},{\mathsf{u}}^{2}
are performed non-interactively using
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{Key}}
. Another
\ell
bits are required for the sharing of
\mathsf{q}
by
P_{0}
.
During online,

exchange
\mathsf{y}_{1},\mathsf{y}_{2}
values in parallel resulting in a communication of

bits and 1 round.

4-input multiplication

For the case of 4-input multiplication with
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{abcd}
, note that

\displaystyle\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{abcd}-\mathsf{r}=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{d}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{d}})-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{abcd}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{abc}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{d}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{abd}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{acd}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bcd}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}\gamma_{\mathsf{cd}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ac}}\gamma_{\mathsf{bd}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ad}}\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bc}}\gamma_{\mathsf{ad}}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ +\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bd}}\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{cd}}\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}\gamma_{\mathsf{bcd}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}\gamma_{\mathsf{acd}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}\gamma_{\mathsf{abd}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{d}}\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{abcd}}-\mathsf{r}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\footnotesize{(cf. notation\penalty\ \ref{notation:3pcSconcise})}}

(3.6)

Here the parties need to generate
\left[\cdot\right]
-shares of
\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}},\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}},\gamma_{\mathsf{ad}},\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}},\gamma_{\mathsf{bd}},\gamma_{\mathsf{cd}},\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}},\gamma_{\mathsf{abd}},\gamma_{\mathsf{acd}},\gamma_{\mathsf{bcd}}
and
\gamma_{\mathsf{abcd}}-\mathsf{r}
. This is computed similarly as in 3-input multiplication and the protocol is denoted as
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult4}}
.

Lemma 3.2.8

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult4}}
(in
ASTRA
) requires
11\ell
bits of communication in the preprocessing, and
1
round and
2\ell
bits of communication in the online phase.

Proof 3.2.9
.

During preprocessing,
\ell
bits of communication from
P_{0}
to
P_{2}
is required to generate
\left[\cdot\right]
-shares of each of the ten values
\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}},\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}},\gamma_{\mathsf{ad}},\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}},\gamma_{\mathsf{bd}},\gamma_{\mathsf{cd}},\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}},\gamma_{\mathsf{abd}},\gamma_{\mathsf{acd}},\gamma_{\mathsf{bcd}}
. The sampling of
{\mathsf{u}}^{1},{\mathsf{u}}^{2}
are performed non-interactively using
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{Key}}
. A communication of
\ell
bits is required for the sharing of
\mathsf{q}
by
P_{0}
.
During online,
P_{1},P_{2}
exchange
\mathsf{y}_{1},\mathsf{y}_{2}
values in parallel resulting in a communication of
2\ell
bits and 1 round.

N
-input multiplication

Consider an
N
-input multiplication gate with inputs
\mathsf{a}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{a}_{N}
and output
\mathsf{z}
. Then, we can write

\mathsf{z-r}=\prod_{j=1}^{N}(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{j}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{j}})-\mathsf{r}=\left(\sum_{I\subseteq\{1,\dots,N\}}(-1)^{|I|}\prod_{j\in I}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{j}}\prod_{k\notin I}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{j}}\right)-\mathsf{r}

(3.7)

Here
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,N\}
denotes a subset of indices from 1 to
N
, while
|I|
denotes the cardinality of the set.

We note that for an
N
-Input multiplication gate, we would require a total of
2^{N}-N-1
terms to be processed in the preprocessing, while the online phase still requires a communication of just
2
ring elements. Hence, to maintain a balance between the online communication and the overhead in the preprocessing, we consider
N=3
and
N=4
in our platform.

3.2.5
Supporting on-demand computations

For on-demand applications where the underlying function to be computed is not known in advance, the preprocessing model is not desirable. We observe that the
ASTRA
protocol can be modified by executing the preprocessing steps in the online phase itself, keeping the same overall communication cost and online rounds. The formal protocol appears in Fig.
3.5
.

\mathsf{isTr}
is a bit denoting whether truncation is required (
\mathsf{isTr}=1
) or not (
\mathsf{isTr}=0
).

Input(s):

\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
.

Output:

\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket
where
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=0
and
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{ab}
.

\justify

Online:

1.

P_{0},P_{j}
sample
{\mathsf{u}}^{j}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
. Let
{\mathsf{u}^{1}}+\mathsf{u}^{2}=\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r}
for
\mathsf{r}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

2.

Let
\mathsf{y}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}
. Compute:
P_{1}:\mathsf{y}_{1}=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+{\mathsf{u}}^{1},\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{2}=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+{\mathsf{u}}^{2}
.

3.

P_{1}
sends
\mathsf{y}_{1}
to
P_{2}
, while
P_{2}
sends
\mathsf{y}_{2}
to
P_{1}
.

4.

Parties proceed as follows:

(a)

P_{0}
:
\mathsf{r}=\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{u}^{1}-\mathsf{u}^{2}
;
\mathsf{q}=\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
, else
\mathsf{q}=\mathsf{r}
. Executes
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}(P_{0},\mathsf{q})
.

(b)

P_{1},P_{2}
:
\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}=(\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{y}_{2})+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}
;
\mathsf{p}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
, else
\mathsf{p}=\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}
. Execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}(P_{1},P_{2},\mathsf{p})
.

5.

Locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket
. Here
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{z}
otherwise.

Figure 3.5
:
Multiplication for on-demand applications in
ASTRA
.

Lemma 3.2.10

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}^{\mathsf{NoPre}}
(Fig.
3.2
) (in
ASTRA
) requires
1
round and
3\ell
bits of communication in the online phase.

Proof 3.2.11
.

Steps 3 and 4 (a) of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}^{\mathsf{NoPre}}
can be executed in parallel resulting in
1
round and
3\ell
bits of communication.

3.3
Garbled World

We propose 2 GC protocols –
ASTRA
T
requiring communication of 2 GCs and 1 online round, and
ASTRA
C
requiring 1 GC and 2 rounds.
The 2 GC variant has two parallel executions, each comprising of 2 garblers and 1 evaluator.
P_{1},P_{2}
act as evaluators in two independent executions and the parties in
\Phi_{1}=\{P_{0},P_{2}\}
,
\Phi_{2}=\{P_{0},P_{1}\}
act as garblers, respectively. The 1 GC variant comprises of a single execution with
\Phi_{1}
acting as garblers and
P_{1}
as the evaluator.

3.3.1
2 GC Variant

Input Phase

Given that the function input
\mathsf{x}
is already available as
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
, the boolean values
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}
act as the
new
inputs for the garbled computation, and garbled sharing (
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
) is generated for each of these values. The semantics of
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
-sharing ensures that each of these shares (
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}
) is available with at least one garbler in each garbling instance. Thus, the goal of our input phase is to create the compound sharing,
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}=({\llbracket\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G})
for every input
\mathsf{x}
to the function to be evaluated via the GC. We first discuss the semantics for
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-sharing followed by steps for generating
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
-sharing.

Garbled sharing semantics

A value
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-shared (garbled shared) amongst
\mathcal{P}
if
P_{0}
holds
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{0}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,2})
,
P_{1}
holds
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{1}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,2})
and
P_{2}
holds
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{2}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},2})
. Here,
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},j}=\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,j}\oplus\mathsf{v}\Delta^{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
, and
\Delta^{j}
, which is known only to the garblers in
\Phi_{j}
, denotes the global offset with its least significant bit set to
1
and is same for every wire in the circuit.
A value
\mathsf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is said to be
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
-shared (compound shared) if each value from
(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}})
is
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-shared. We write
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}=({\llbracket\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G})
.

Generation of
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
and
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}

Protocol
\mathrm{\Pi}_{\mathsf{Sh}}^{\bf G}(\mathcal{P},\mathsf{v})
(Fig.
3.6
) enables generation of
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
where two garblers in each garbling instance hold
\mathsf{v}
, and proceeds as follows. Consider the first garbling instance with evaluator
P_{1}
. Garblers in
\Phi_{1}
generate
\{\mathsf{K}_{{{\mathsf{v}}}}^{\mathsf{b},1}\}_{\mathsf{b}\in\{0,1\}}
which denotes the key for value
\mathsf{b}
on wire
\mathsf{v}
, following the free-XOR technique
[
82
,
84
]
.
P_{s}\in\Phi_{1}
sends
\mathsf{K}_{{{\mathsf{v}}}}^{\mathsf{v},1}
to evaluator
P_{1}
where
P_{s}\in\Phi_{1}
denotes the garbler that knows
\mathsf{v}
in clear.
Similar steps carried out with respect to the second garbling instance, at the end of which, garblers in
\Phi_{2}
possess
\{\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{b},2}\}_{\mathsf{b}\in\{0,1\}}
while the evaluator
P_{2}
holds
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},2}
. Following this, the shares
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{s}
held by
P_{s}\in\mathcal{P}
are defined as
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{0}=(\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{0,2})
,
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{1}=(\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},1},\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{0,2})
,
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{2}=(\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},2})
.
To generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
,
\mathrm{\Pi}_{\mathsf{Sh}}^{\bf G}
is invoked for each of
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}
and
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}
.

\justify

Input(s):

\mathsf{v}
,
Output:

{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
.

Let
P_{s}\in\Phi_{j}
be the garbler that knows
\mathsf{v}
in clear where
j\in\{1,2\}
.

1.

Garblers in
\Phi_{j}
generate keys
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,j},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{1,j}
for wire
\mathsf{v}
, using free-XOR technique.

2.

P_{s}\in\Phi_{j}
sends
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},j}
to evaluator
P_{j}
for the
j^{\text{th}}
garbling instance.

3.

P_{0}
sets
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{0}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,2})
,
P_{1}
sets
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{1}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,2})
and
P_{2}
sets
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{2}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},2})
.

Figure 3.6
:
Generation of
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
in
ASTRA
.

Evaluation

Let
f(\mathsf{x})
be the function to be evaluated. At this point, the function input is
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
-shared. This renders
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-sharing for the input of the GC that corresponds to the function
f^{\prime}\big({\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}},{\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}}}\big)
which first combines the given boolean-shares to compute the actual input and then applies
f
on it. Let
\mathsf{GC}_{j}
denote the garbled circuit to be sent to
P_{j}\in\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
by garblers in
\Phi_{j}
. Sending of
\mathsf{GC}_{j}
is overlapped with the key transfer (during generation of
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
), to save rounds, where garbler
P_{0}
sends
\mathsf{GC}_{j}
to
P_{j}
. On receiving the
\mathsf{GC}
, evaluators evaluate their respective GCs and obtain the key corresponding to the output, say
\mathsf{z}
. This generates
{\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
.

Output phase

The goal of output computation is to compute the output
\mathsf{z}
from
{\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
.
To reconstruct
\mathsf{z}
towards
P_{j}\in\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
,
P_{0}
sends the least significant bit
\mathsf{p}^{j}
of
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{z}}^{0,j}
, referred to as the decoding information, to
P_{j}
.
P_{j}
uses the received
\mathsf{p}^{j}
to reconstruct
\mathsf{z}
as
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{p}^{j}\oplus\mathsf{q}^{j}
, where
\mathsf{q}^{j}
denotes the least significant bit of
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\mathsf{z},j}
.
To reconstruct
\mathsf{z}
towards
P_{0}
, one evaluator, say
P_{1}
sends the least significant bit,
\mathsf{q}^{1}
, of
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{\mathsf{z},1}
to
P_{0}
. Reconstruction is lightweight and requires a single round for garblers while reconstruction towards evaluators can be overlapped with key transfer and does not incur extra rounds.
The protocol appears in Fig.
3.7
.

\justify

Input(s):

{\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
,
Output:

\mathsf{z}
.

1.

For an output wire
\mathsf{z}
, let
\mathsf{p}^{j}
denote the least significant bit of
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{0,j}
and
\mathsf{q}^{j}
denote the least significant bit of
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{\mathsf{z},j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
.

2.

Reconstruction towards
P_{j}\in\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
:
P_{0}
sends
\mathsf{p}^{j}
to
P_{j}
who reconstructs
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{p}^{j}\oplus\mathsf{q}^{j}
.

3.

Reconstruction towards
P_{0}
:
P_{1}
(or
P_{2}
) sends
\mathsf{q}^{1}
to
P_{0}
who reconstructs
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{p}^{1}\oplus\mathsf{q}^{1}
.

Figure 3.7
:
Output computation: reconstruction of
\mathsf{z}
in
ASTRA
.

Optimizations when deployed in mixed framework

Working in the preprocessing model enables transfer of the (communication-intensive) GC and generating
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-shares of the input-independent shares of
\mathsf{x}
(i.e.
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}
) in the preprocessing. Thus, the online phase is very light and only requires one round to generate
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-shares for the input-dependent data (i.e.
{\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}}
). Since evaluation is local, evaluators obtain
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-sharing of the GC output at the end of
1
round. Moreover, we require the garbled output to be reconstructed towards both
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
in clear. Thus, the steps for reconstruction towards
P_{0}
can be avoided in
\mathrm{\Pi}_{\mathsf{Rec}}^{\bf G}
protocol (Fig.
3.7
).

3.3.2
1 GC Variant

The garbling scheme here is similar to the 2GC variant except that now there exists only a single garbling instance. Parties in
\Phi_{1}=\{P_{0},P_{2}\}
act as the garblers while
P_{1}
act as the evaluator. Looking ahead, in the mixed protocol framework, the output has to be reconstructed towards
P_{1},P_{2}
. Reconstruction towards
P_{1}
does not incur additional rounds since sending of decoding information can be overlapped with the key transfer. However, unlike in the 2GC variant, an additional round is required for
P_{1}
to send the output to
P_{2}
. This incurs one extra round as opposed to the 2GC variant.

3.4
Security proofs

The simulation for the semi-honest 3PC case is straightforward in the
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{setup}}
-hybrid model, where
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{setup}}
(§
2.5.1
) denotes the ideal functionality for the shared-key setup. The strategy for simulating the computation of function
f
(represented by a circuit
\mathsf{Ckt}
) is as follows. The simulation begins with the simulator emulating the shared-key setup (
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{setup}}
) functionality and giving the respective keys to the adversary
\mathcal{A}
. Since
\mathcal{S}
is given the input and output of the
\mathcal{A}
, it can compute all the intermediate values of the circuit
\mathsf{Ckt}
in clear.

For the input sharing of value
\mathsf{v}
,
\mathcal{S}
receives the
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
from
\mathcal{A}
on behalf of the honest parties. Similarly, for the inputs of honest parties,
\mathcal{S}
interacts with the
\mathcal{A}
with the inputs set to
0
. The simulated view is indistinguishable from the ideal view due to the privacy of the underlying sharing scheme. The linear gates involve no communication, while simulation of the multiplication protocol is straightforward. Moreover, simulation for the joint sharing (
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
) instances is similar to that of the sharing protocol. The protocol’s design is such that
\mathcal{S}
will always know the value to be sent as part of the joint sharing protocol. Finally, for the reconstruction towards
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{S}
calculates the missing share of
\mathcal{A}
using
\mathsf{y}
and the other shares. The missing share is then communicated to
\mathcal{A}
as per the reconstruction protocol.

Chapter 4
SWIFT
: 3PC Fair and Robust Protocols

This chapter provides details for the Layer I blocks of our 3PC framework
SWIFT
. Some of the results in this chapter resulted in publications at NDSS’20
[
110
]
and USENIX Security’21
[
85
]
. Comparison of
SWIFT
with actively secure 3PC PPML framework of ABY3
[
101
]
, in terms of the communication for multiplication, is presented in Table
4.1
.

†
†
nicematrix-placeholder:
NiceTabular (nicematrix)

Table 4.1
:
Comparison of malicious 3PC frameworks for PPML

4.1
Preliminaries and Definitions

We consider
3
parties denoted by
\mathcal{P}=\{P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}\}
that are connected by pair-wise private and authentic channels in a synchronous network, and a static, malicious adversary that can corrupt at most 1 party.

4.1.1
Sharing Semantics

For the arithmetic and boolean sharing, we follow a
(3,1)
RSS scheme similar to
ASTRA
, except that a value
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
is split into four shares. Three of the shares (
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3})
can be generated in the preprocessing phase independent of the value to be shared, and their sum can be interpreted as a mask (
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
). The fourth share, dependent on
\mathsf{v}
, can be computed in the online phase and can be treated as the masked value
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
.

Similar to
ASTRA
, we distinguish the three parties into two sets; the
eval
set
\mathcal{E}=\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
which is assigned the task of carrying out the computation, and is active throughout the online phase. The
helper
set
\mathcal{D}=\{P_{3}\}
, is used to assist
\mathcal{E}
in verification, and so it is only active towards the end of the computation. Moreover, the share distribution is done as follows:
P_{1}:\{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}\},P_{2}:\{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}\}
, and
P_{3}:\{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}\}
.

Table 4.2
:
Semantics for
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
in
SWIFT
.
†
†
nicematrix-placeholder:
NiceTabular (nicematrix)

The RSS sharing semantics is presented in Table
4.2
, denoted by
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
, in a modular way with the help of two intermediate sharing semantics
\left[\cdot\right]
, and
\langle\cdot\rangle
. All the sharings used are linear i.e. given sharings of values
\mathsf{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{v}_{m}
and public constants
c_{1},\ldots,c_{m}
, sharing of
\sum_{i=1}^{m}c_{i}\mathsf{v}_{i}
can be computed non-interactively for an integer
m
.

Notation 4.1.1

(a) For the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of
n
values
\mathsf{a}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{a}_{n}
,
\gamma_{\mathsf{a}_{1}\ldots\mathsf{a}_{n}}=\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}
and
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{1}\ldots\mathsf{a}_{n}}{}=\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}{}
(b) We use superscripts
{\bf B}
, and
{\bf G}
to denote sharing semantics in boolean, and garbled world, respectively–
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
,
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
. We omit the superscript for arithmetic world.

Sharing semantics for boolean sharing over
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is similar to arithmetic sharing except that addition is replaced with XOR. The semantics for garbled sharing are described in §
4.3
with the relevant context.

4.1.1.1
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{zero}}
- Generating additive shares of zero

In
SWIFT
, we make use of a functionality
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{zero}}
to enable
P_{i}
obtain
Z_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
such that
Z_{1}+Z_{2}+Z_{3}=0
. We observe that the functionality can be instantiated non-interactively using the pre-shared keys (cf. §
2.5.1
). For this, parties in
\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{j}\}
sample random value
\mathsf{r}_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
. The shares are then defined as
Z_{1}=\mathsf{r}_{3}-\mathsf{r}_{2},Z_{2}=\mathsf{r}_{1}-\mathsf{r}_{3}
and
Z_{3}=\mathsf{r}_{2}-\mathsf{r}_{1}
.

4.1.2
Joint-Send (
\mathsf{jsnd}
) Primitive

The Joint-Send (
\mathsf{jsnd}
) primitive, for the case of security with fairness, allows parties
P_{i},P_{j}
to relay a message
\mathsf{v}
to a third party
P_{k}
ensuring either the delivery of the message or
\mathtt{abort}
in case of inconsistency. Towards this,
P_{i}
sends
\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
, while
P_{j}
sends a hash of the same (
\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{v})
) to
P_{k}
. Party
P_{k}
accepts the message if the hash values are consistent and
\mathtt{abort}
otherwise. Note that the communication of the hash can be clubbed together for several instances and be deferred to the end of the protocol, amortizing the cost.

Joint-Send (
\mathsf{jsnd}
) for robust protocols

The
\mathsf{jsnd}
primitive, for the case of robustness, allows
P_{i},P_{j}
to relay a common message,
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
, to recipient
P_{k}
, either by ensuring successful delivery of
\mathsf{v}
, or by establishing a Trusted Third Party (
\mathsf{TTP}
). The striking feature of
\mathsf{jsnd}
is that it offers a rate-
1
communication, i.e. for a message of
\ell
elements, it only incurs a communication of
\ell
elements (in an amortized sense). The task of
\mathsf{jsnd}
is captured in an ideal functionality (Fig.
4.1
) and the protocol for the same appears in Fig.
4.2
. Next, we give an overview.

\justify

\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{jsnd}$}}
interacts with the parties in
\mathcal{P}
and the adversary
\mathcal{S}
.

Step 1:

\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{jsnd}$}}
receives
(\mathsf{Input},\mathsf{v}_{s})
from
P_{s}
for
s\in\{i,j\}
, while it receives
(\mathsf{select},\mathsf{ttp})
from
\mathcal{S}
.
\mathsf{ttp}
denotes the party that
\mathcal{S}
wants to choose as the
\mathsf{TTP}
and
P^{\star}\in\mathcal{P}
denotes the corrupt party.

Step 2:

If
\mathsf{v}_{i}=\mathsf{v}_{j}
and
\mathsf{ttp}=\bot
, then set
\mathsf{msg}_{i}=\mathsf{msg}_{j}=\bot,\mathsf{msg}_{k}=\mathsf{v}_{i}
and go to
Step 5
.

Step 3:

If
\mathsf{ttp}\in\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P^{\star}\}
, then set
\mathsf{msg}_{i}=\mathsf{msg}_{j}=\mathsf{msg}_{k}=\mathsf{ttp}
and go to
Step 5
.

Step 4:

\mathsf{TTP}
is the honest party with smallest index. Set
\mathsf{msg}_{i}=\mathsf{msg}_{j}=\mathsf{msg}_{k}=\mathsf{TTP}

Step 5:

Send
(\mathsf{Output},\mathsf{msg}_{s})
to
P_{s}
for
s\in\{1,2,3\}
.

Figure 4.1
:
Ideal functionality for robust
\mathsf{jsnd}
primitive in
SWIFT

Given two parties
P_{i},P_{j}
possessing a common value
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}
proceeds as follows. First,
P_{i}
sends
\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
while
P_{j}
sends a hash of
\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
. The communication of the hash is done once and for all from
P_{j}
to
P_{k}
. In the simplest case,
P_{k}
receives a consistent (value, hash) pair, and the protocol terminates. In all other cases, a
\mathsf{TTP}
is identified as follows without having to communicate
\mathsf{v}
again. Importantly, the following part can be run once and for all instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}
with
P_{i},P_{j},P_{k}
in the same roles, invoked in the final 3PC protocol. Consequently, the cost relevant to this part vanishes in an amortized sense, making the construction rate-1.

Input(s):

P_{i},P_{j}:\mathsf{v}
,
P_{k}:\bot
,
Output:

P_{i},P_{j}:\bot/\mathsf{TTP}
,
P_{k}:\mathsf{v}/\mathsf{TTP}
.

Each party
P_{s}
for
s\in\{i,j,k\}
initializes bit
\mathsf{b}_{s}=0
.

\justify
Send:

P_{i}
sends
\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
.

Verify:

P_{j}
sends
\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{v})
to
P_{k}
.

–

P_{k}
broadcasts
“
(accuse,
\mathtt{P_{i}}
)
”
, if
P_{i}
is silent and
\mathsf{TTP}
=
P_{j}
. Analogously for
P_{j}
. If
P_{k}
accuses both
P_{i},P_{j}
, then
\mathsf{TTP}
=
P_{i}
. Otherwise,
P_{k}
receives some
\tilde{\mathsf{v}}
and either sets
\mathsf{b}_{k}=0
when the value and the hash are consistent or sets
\mathsf{b}_{k}=1
.
P_{k}
then sends
\mathsf{b}_{k}
to
P_{i},P_{j}
and terminates if
\mathsf{b}_{k}=0
.

–

If
P_{i}
does not receive a bit from
P_{k}
, it broadcasts
“
(accuse,
\mathtt{P_{k}}
)
”
and
\mathsf{TTP}
=
P_{j}
. Analogously for
P_{j}
. If both
P_{i},P_{j}
accuse
P_{k}
, then
\mathsf{TTP}
=
P_{i}
. Otherwise,
P_{s}
for
s\in\{i,j\}
sets
\mathsf{b}_{s}=\mathsf{b}_{k}
.

–

P_{i},P_{j}
exchange their bits to each other. If
P_{i}
does not receive
\mathsf{b}_{j}
from
P_{j}
, it broadcasts
“
(accuse,
\mathtt{P_{j}}
)
”
and
\mathsf{TTP}
=
P_{k}
. Analogously for
P_{j}
. Otherwise,
P_{i}
resets its bit to
\mathsf{b}_{i}\vee\mathsf{b}_{j}
and likewise
P_{j}
resets its bit to
\mathsf{b}_{j}\vee\mathsf{b}_{i}
.

–

P_{s}
for
s\in\{i,j,k\}
broadcasts
\mathsf{H}_{s}=\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{v}^{*})
if
b_{s}=1
, where
\mathsf{v}^{*}=\mathsf{v}
for
s\in\{i,j\}
and
\mathsf{v}^{*}=\tilde{\mathsf{v}}
otherwise. If
P_{k}
does not broadcast, terminate. If either
P_{i}
or
P_{j}
does not broadcast, then
\mathsf{TTP}
=
P_{k}
. Otherwise,

\bullet

If
\mathsf{H}_{i}\neq\mathsf{H}_{j}
:
\mathsf{TTP}
=
P_{k}
.

\bullet

Else if
\mathsf{H}_{i}\neq\mathsf{H}_{k}
:
\mathsf{TTP}
=
P_{j}
.

\bullet

Else if
\mathsf{H}_{i}=\mathsf{H}_{j}=\mathsf{H}_{k}
:
\mathsf{TTP}
=
P_{i}
.

Figure 4.2
:
Joint-Send for robust protocols in
SWIFT

Each
P_{s}
for
s\in\{i,j,k\}
maintains a bit
\mathsf{b}_{s}
initialized to
0
, as an indicator for inconsistency. When
P_{k}
receives an inconsistent (value, hash) pair, it sets
\mathsf{b}_{k}=1
and sends the bit to both
P_{i},P_{j}
. Parties
P_{i},P_{j}
cross-check with each other by exchanging the bit and turning on their inconsistency bit if the bit received from either
P_{k}
or its fellow sender is turned on. A party broadcasts a hash of its value when its inconsistency bit is on;
1
1

1

hash can be computed on a combined message across many calls of
\mathsf{jsnd}
.

P_{k}
’s value is the one it receives from
P_{i}
. There are a bunch of possible cases at this stage, and a detailed analysis determines an eligible
\mathsf{TTP}
in each case.

When
P_{k}
is silent, the protocol is understood to be complete. This is fine irrespective of the status of
P_{k}
– an honest
P_{k}
never skips this broadcast with inconsistency bit on, and a corrupt
P_{k}
implies honest senders. If either
P_{i}
or
P_{j}
is silent, then
P_{k}
is picked as
\mathsf{TTP}
which is surely honest. A corrupt
P_{k}
could not make one of
\{P_{i},P_{j}\}
speak, as the senders (honest in this case) agree on their inconsistency bit (due to their mutual exchange of inconsistency bit). When all of them speak and (i) the senders’ hashes do not match,
P_{k}
is picked as
\mathsf{TTP}
; (ii) one of the senders conflicts with
P_{k}
, the other sender is picked as
\mathsf{TTP}
; and lastly (iii) if there is no conflict,
P_{i}
is picked as
\mathsf{TTP}
. The first two cases are self-explanatory. In the last case, either
P_{j}
or
P_{k}
is corrupt. If not, a corrupt
P_{i}
can have honest
P_{k}
speak (and hence turn on its inconsistency bit) by sending a
\mathsf{v}^{\prime}
whose hash is not the same as that of
\mathsf{v}
and so inevitably, the hashes of honest
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
will conflict, contradicting (iii).
As a final touch, we ensure that, in each step, a party raises a public alarm (via broadcast) accusing a silent party when it is not supposed to be. Then the protocol terminates immediately by labelling the party as
\mathsf{TTP}
who is neither the complainer nor the accused.

Using
\mathsf{jsnd}
in protocols.
As mentioned earlier, the
\mathsf{jsnd}
protocol needs to be viewed as consisting of two phases (
send, verify
), where
send
phase consists of
P_{i}
sending
\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
and the rest goes to
verify
phase. Looking ahead, most of our protocols use
\mathsf{jsnd}
, and consequently, our final construction, either of general MPC or any PPML task, will have several calls to
\mathsf{jsnd}
. To leverage amortization, the
send
phase will be executed in all protocols invoking
\mathsf{jsnd}
on the flow, while the
verify
for a fixed ordered pair of senders will be executed once and for all in the end. The
verify
phase will determine if all the sends were correct. If not, a
\mathsf{TTP}
is identified, as explained, and the computation completes with the help of
\mathsf{TTP}
, just as in the ideal world.

Lemma 4.1.2

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}
(Fig.
4.2
) requires
1
round and an amortized communication of
\ell
bits overall.

Proof 4.1.3
.

Party
P_{i}
sends value
\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
while
P_{j}
sends hash of the same to
P_{k}
. This accounts for one round and communication of
\ell
bits.
P_{k}
then sends back its inconsistency bit to
P_{i},P_{j}
, who then exchange it; this takes another two rounds. This is followed by parties broadcasting hashes on their values and selecting a
\mathsf{TTP}
based on it, which takes one more round. All except the first round can be combined for several instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}
protocol and hence the cost gets amortized.

Note that the appropriate instantiation of
\mathsf{jsnd}
is used depending on the security guarantee. For simplicity, protocols where the fair and robust variants only differ in the instantiation of
\mathsf{jsnd}
used, we give a common construction for both.

Notation 4.1.4

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}
denotes the instantiation of Joint-Send (
\mathsf{jsnd}
) primitive. We say that
P_{i},P_{j}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
when they invoke
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}(P_{i},P_{j},\mathsf{v},P_{k})
.

4.2
Arithmetic / Boolean 3PC

This section covers the details of our 3PC protocol
SWIFT
over an arithmetic ring
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
. We begin by explaining the sharing protocol in §
4.2.1
, multiplication with abort in §
4.2.2
, and the reconstruction in §
4.2.3
. Lastly, the details on elevating the security to fairness are presented in §
4.2.3.1
and to robustness in §
4.2.4
.

4.2.1
Sharing

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
(Fig.
4.3
) enables
P_{i}
to generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-share of a value
\mathsf{v}
. During the preprocessing phase,
\mathsf{\lambda}
-shares are sampled non-interactively using the pre-shared keys (cf. §
2.5.1
) in a way that
P_{i}
will get the entire mask
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
. During the online phase,
P_{i}
computes
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
and sends to
P_{1}
. Parties
P_{i},P_{1}
then communicates
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{2}
and
P_{3}
using
\mathsf{jsnd}
primitive.

Input(s):

P_{i}:\mathsf{v}
,
Output:

\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

Sample as follows:
P_{i},P_{1},P_{3}:\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}
,
P_{i},P_{2},P_{3}:\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}
,
P_{i},P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}
.

\justify

Online:

1.

P_{i}
computes
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
and sends to
P_{j}
. Here
P_{j}=P_{1}
if
P_{i}\neq P_{1}
, else
P_{j}=P_{2}
.

2.

P_{i},P_{j}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{2}
and
P_{3}
.

Figure 4.3
:
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of a value
\mathsf{v}
by party
P_{i}
in
SWIFT
.

For the case when sharing happens in the preprocessing, the communication can be optimized to
\ell
bits. For this, parties set
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=0
and the
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
-shares are computed as follows:

–

P_{i}=P_{1}
:
\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{2}\}\leftarrow_{R}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}
;
\mathcal{P}\leftarrow_{R}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}
;
P_{1}
sends
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}=-(\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2})
to
P_{2}
.

–

P_{i}=P_{2}
:
\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{1}\}\leftarrow_{R}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}
;
\mathcal{P}\leftarrow_{R}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}
;
P_{2}
sends
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}=-(\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2})
to
P_{1}
.

–

P_{i}=P_{3}
:
\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{1}\}\leftarrow_{R}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}
;
\mathcal{P}\leftarrow_{R}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}
;
P_{3}
sends
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}=-(\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3})
to
P_{1}
.

Lemma 4.2.5

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
(Fig.
4.3
) requires an amortized communication of at most
2\ell
bits and
2
rounds in the online phase.

Proof 4.2.6
.

The preprocessing of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
is non-interactive as the parties sample non interactively using key setup
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{Setup}}
(§
2.5.1
). In the online phase,
P_{i}
sends
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{1}
resulting in 1 round and communication of
\ell
bits. The next round consists of one instance of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}
protocol and the cost follows from Lemma
4.1.2
.

4.2.1.1
Joint Sharing

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
enables parties
P_{i},P_{j}
to generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-share of a value
\mathsf{v}
. The protocol is similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
except that
P_{j}
ensures the correctness of the sharing performed by
P_{i}
. During the preprocessing,
\mathsf{\lambda}
-shares are sampled such that both
P_{i},P_{j}
will get the entire mask
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
. During the online phase,
P_{i},P_{j}
compute and
\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
to parties
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
.

When the value
\mathsf{v}
is available to both
P_{i},P_{j}
in the preprocessing, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
can be made non-interactive by setting the shares as given in Table
4.3
.

Table 4.3
:
Shares for
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
in the preprocessing in
SWIFT
.
†
†
nicematrix-placeholder:
NiceTabular (nicematrix)

Lemma 4.2.7

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
is non-interactive in the preprocessing and requires an amortized communication of
\ell
bits and
1
round in the online phase.

Proof 4.2.8
.

The protocol involves one invocation of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}
protocol in the online and the cost follows from Lemma
4.1.2
.

4.2.2
Multiplication

Given the shares of
\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b}
, the goal of the multiplication protocol is to generate shares of
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{ab}
. The protocol is designed such that parties
P_{1},P_{2}
obtain a masked version of the output
\mathsf{z}
, say
\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}
in the online phase. Moreover, parties obtain the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of the mask
\mathsf{r}
in the preprocessing.
P_{1},P_{2}
then generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of
(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})
by executing
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
. Parties locally compute the final output as
\llbracket\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{r}\rrbracket
.

Online

Similar to
ASTRA
, we have,

\displaystyle\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}-\mathsf{r}=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}})-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{r}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\footnotesize{(cf. notation\penalty\ \ref{notation:3pcMconcise})}}

(4.1)

In Eq
4.2.2
, all the parties can compute
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}
locally, and hence we are interested in computing
\mathsf{y}=(\mathsf{z-r})-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}
. Let
\mathsf{y}=\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{y}_{3}
, where
\mathsf{y}_{1},\mathsf{y}_{2},\mathsf{y}_{3}
can be computed respectively by the pairs
(P_{1},P_{3}),(P_{2},P_{3})
and
(P_{1},P_{2})
. Given a preprocessing that enables parties to obtain a
\langle\cdot\rangle
-sharing of
(\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r})
, parties locally compute the additive shares of
\mathsf{y}
according to (
4.2.2
).

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+{(\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r})}^{1}

\displaystyle P_{2},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+{(\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r})}^{2}

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+{(\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r})}^{3}

(4.2)

Once the shares are computed,
P_{1},P_{3}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{y}_{1}
to
P_{2}
and
P_{2},P_{3}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{y}_{2}
to
P_{1}
. Parties
P_{1},P_{2}
reconstruct
\mathsf{y}
using the shares received and subsequently
\mathsf{z-r}
.

\mathsf{isTr}
is a bit denoting whether truncation is required (
\mathsf{isTr}=1
) or not (
\mathsf{isTr}=0
).

Input(s):

\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
.

Output:

\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket
where
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=0
and
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{ab}
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

1.

Invoke
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{MultPre}$}}
on
\langle\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}\rangle
and
\langle\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}\rangle
to obtain
\langle\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}\rangle
.

2.

If
\mathsf{isTr}=0
:

(a)

Local computation of
\langle\mathsf{r}\rangle
:
\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{2}\}\leftarrow_{R}{\mathsf{r}}^{1}
;
\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{1}\}\leftarrow_{R}{\mathsf{r}}^{2}
;
\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{3}\}\leftarrow_{R}{\mathsf{r}}^{3}
.

(b)

Local computation of
\llbracket\mathsf{r}\rrbracket
:
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{r}}^{1}=-{\mathsf{r}}^{1}
,
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{r}}^{2}=-{\mathsf{r}}^{2}
,
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{r}}^{3}=-{\mathsf{r}}^{3}
,
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{r}}=0
. Set
\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{r}\rrbracket
.

3.

If
\mathsf{isTr}=1
, invoke
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
(Fig.
8.4
) to generate
(\langle\mathsf{r}\rangle,\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket)
. Set
\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket
.

4.

Locally compute
\langle(\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r})\rangle=\langle\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}\rangle-\langle\mathsf{r}\rangle
.

\justify

Online:

Let
\mathsf{y}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}
.

1.

Parties locally compute the following:

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+{(\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r})}^{1}

\displaystyle P_{2},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+{(\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r})}^{2}

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+{(\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r})}^{3}

2.

P_{1},P_{3}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{y}_{1}
to
P_{2}
, while
P_{2},P_{3}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{y}_{2}
to
P_{1}
. They locally compute
\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}=(\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{y}_{3})+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}
.

3.

P_{1},P_{2}
: If
\mathsf{isTr}=1
, set
\mathsf{p}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})^{\mathsf{t}}
, else
\mathsf{p}=\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}
. Execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}(P_{1},P_{2},\mathsf{p})
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket
.

4.

Compute
\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket
. Here
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{z}
otherwise.

Figure 4.4
:
Multiplication with / without truncation in
SWIFT
.

Verification

To leverage amortization, the
send
phase of
\mathsf{jsnd}
alone is executed on the fly and
verify
is performed once for multiple instances of
\mathsf{jsnd}
. Further, observe that
P_{1},P_{2}
possess the required shares in the online phase to compute the entire circuit. Hence,
P_{3}
can come in only during
verify
of
\mathsf{jsnd}
towards
P_{1},P_{2}
, which can be deferred towards the end. Hence, the
\mathsf{jsnd}
to
P_{3}
(as part of
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
by
P_{1},P_{2}
during the online) can be performed once, towards the end, thereby requiring a single round for multiple instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
. Following this, the
verify
of
\mathsf{jsnd}
towards
P_{3}
is performed first, followed by performing the
verify
of
\mathsf{jsnd}
towards
P_{1},P_{2}
in parallel.

Preprocessing

As mentioned above, parties should obtain a
\langle\cdot\rangle
-sharing of
(\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r})
from the preprocessing. The
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares for a random
\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
can be generated non-interactively using the key setup
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{Setup}}
(§
2.5.1
). To compute
\langle\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}\rangle
, we rely on a 3-party multiplication protocol, say
\Pi_{\mathsf{MultPre}}
, abstracted in a functionality
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{MultPre}$}}
(Fig.
4.5
). The security of
\Pi_{\mathsf{MultPre}}
depends on the security required in our framework. For instance, instantiating
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{MultPre}$}}
with the protocols of
[
24
]
and
[
2
]
will result in abort or fairness guarantees whereas using the robust 3 party protocol of
[
27
]
will result in a multiplication protocol with robustness. In
SWIFT
, we use the protocol of
[
27
]
in a black-box manner resulting in a communication of
3\ell
bits (amortized) for
\Pi_{\mathsf{MultPre}}
. This leaves room for further improvements in the overall efficiency of our multiplication, which can be obtained by instantiating the black-box with efficient protocols.

\justify

\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{MultPre}$}}
interacts with the parties in
\mathcal{P}
and the adversary
\mathcal{S}
.
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{MultPre}$}}
receives
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of
\mathsf{d},\mathsf{e}
from the parties. Let
P^{\star}
denotes the party corrupted by
\mathcal{S}
.
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{MultPre}$}}
receives
(\mathsf{f}_{i},\mathsf{f}_{j})
from
\mathcal{S}
as its share for
\langle\mathsf{f}\rangle
where
\mathsf{f}=\mathsf{d}\mathsf{e}
.
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{MultPre}$}}
proceeds as follows:

1.

Reconstructs
\mathsf{d},\mathsf{e}
using the shares received from honest parties and compute
\mathsf{f}=\mathsf{d}\mathsf{e}
.

2.

Computes the third share
\mathsf{f}_{k}=\mathsf{f}-\mathsf{f}_{i}-\mathsf{f}_{j}
and sets
\langle\mathsf{f}\rangle_{1}=(\mathsf{f}_{1},\mathsf{f}_{3}),\langle\mathsf{f}\rangle_{2}=(\mathsf{f}_{2},\mathsf{f}_{3}),\langle\mathsf{f}\rangle_{3}=(\mathsf{f}_{1},\mathsf{f}_{2})
.

3.

Send
(\mathsf{Output},\langle\mathsf{f}\rangle_{s})
to
P_{s}\in\mathcal{P}
.

Figure 4.5
:
Ideal functionality for
\Pi_{\mathsf{MultPre}}
in
SWIFT
.

Lemma 4.2.9

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
(Fig.
4.4
) without truncation (in
SWIFT
) requires
3\ell
bits of communication in the preprocessing, and
1
round and
3\ell
bits of communication in the online phase.

Proof 4.2.10
.

During preprocessing, sampling of the shares for
\langle\mathsf{r}\rangle
is performed non-interactively using
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{Setup}}
. The
\Pi_{\mathsf{MultPre}}
protocol, instantiated using the protocol of
[
27
]
requires a communication of
3\ell
bits in the preprocessing.
During online, two instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}
are executed in parallel resulting in a communication of
2\ell
bits and 1 round. This is followed by a joint sharing by
P_{1},P_{2}
for which an additional communication of
\ell
bits are required. However, in joint sharing, the communication is from
P_{1}
to
P_{3}
and the same can be deferred till the verification stage. Thus the online round is retained as
1
in an amortized sense.

4.2.2.1
Truncation

To incorporate truncation, the multiplication protocol is modified such that
P_{1},P_{2}
execute joint sharing on the truncated value of
(\mathsf{z-r})
in the online phase. To complete the protocol, the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of the truncated
\mathsf{r}
, denoted by
\mathsf{r}^{t}
, is needed. For this, we use
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
(Fig.
8.4
) protocol in the preprocessing that generates a pair of the form
(\langle\mathsf{r}\rangle,\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket)
. More details on
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
are provided in §
8.1.5
. Parties locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket=\llbracket(\mathsf{z-r})^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket
.

4.2.2.2
Multiplication with constant

Multiplication by a constant in MPC is typically local. Given constant
\alpha
and
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
, the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of the product
\mathsf{y}=\alpha\mathsf{v}
can be locally computed as per (
4.3
).

\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}}=\alpha\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{u}},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{1}=\alpha\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{2}=\alpha\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{3}=\alpha\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}

(4.3)

In FPA, parties should obtain truncated
\mathsf{y}
as both
\alpha
and
\mathsf{v}
are decimal values. For this, parties invoke
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
(Fig.
8.4
) in the preprocessing to generate
(\langle\mathsf{r}\rangle,\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket)
for a random
\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
. The
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of
\mathsf{r}
are locally computed from
\langle\mathsf{r}\rangle
locally similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
(Fig.
4.4
). During online, parties locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{v-r}\rrbracket
and reconstructs
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{v-r}
using
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}
(Fig.
4.6
). Parties locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket
by setting
\mathsf{m}_{{\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}}}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
and
\mathsf{\lambda}_{{\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}}}^{1}=\mathsf{\lambda}_{{\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}}}^{2}=\mathsf{\lambda}_{{\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}}}^{3}=0
. Lastly, parties locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{v}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket-\llbracket\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket
.

4.2.3
Reconstruction

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}(\mathcal{P},\mathsf{v})
(Fig.
4.6
) enables parties to compute
\mathsf{v}
, given its
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-share and achieves security with abort. Note that each party misses one share to reconstruct the output, and the other two parties hold this share. They will
\mathsf{jsnd}
(abort variant) the missing share to the party that lacks it. Reconstruction towards a single party can be viewed as a special case.

\justify

Input(s):

\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
,
Output:

\mathsf{v}
.

1.

P_{1},P_{3}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}
to
P_{2}
;
P_{2},P_{3}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}
to
P_{1}
;
P_{1},P_{2}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}
to
P_{3}
.

2.

Parties compute
\mathsf{v}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}
.

Figure 4.6
:
Reconstruction (with abort security) of value
\mathsf{v}
among
\mathcal{P}
in
SWIFT
.

Lemma 4.2.11

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Rec}}
(abort security, Fig.
4.6
) requires an amortized communication of
3\ell
bits and
1
round.

Proof 4.2.12
.

The protocol involves three invocations of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}
protocol and the cost follows from Lemma
4.1.2
.

\justify

Input(s):

\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
,
Output:

\mathsf{v}
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

1.

Parties locally compute the commitments on the
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
shares as:

P_{1},P_{3}:\mathsf{Com}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}),\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{2},P_{3}:\mathsf{Com}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}),\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{Com}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3})

2.

P_{1},P_{3}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{Com}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1})
to
P_{2}
;
P_{2},P_{3}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{Com}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2})
to
P_{1}
;
P_{1},P_{2}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{Com}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3})
to
P_{3}

\justify

Online:

Parties set their aliveness bit
\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{continue}
, if the verification phase is successful. Else
\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{abort}
.

1.

Party
P_{s}\in\mathcal{P}
broadcasts
\mathsf{b}_{s}
and parties accept the value that forms the majority.

2.

If the accepted value is
\mathtt{abort}
, parties abort. Else
P_{1},P_{3}
open
\mathsf{Com}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1})
towards
P_{2}
;
P_{2},P_{3}
open
\mathsf{Com}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2})
towards
P_{1}
;
P_{1},P_{2}
open
\mathsf{Com}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3})
towards
P_{3}
. Parties use the correct opening to obtain their missing share.

3.

Parties compute
\mathsf{v}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}
.

Figure 4.7
:
Fair Reconstruction of value
\mathsf{v}
among
\mathcal{P}
in
SWIFT
.

4.2.3.1
Achieving Fairness

Here, we show how to extend the security of
SWIFT
from abort to fairness by modifying the reconstruction protocol. During preprocessing, each pair of parties together prepare a commitment on the
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
share missing at the third party. The commitments are then communicated via
\mathsf{jsnd}
(abort variant), and the privacy is guaranteed by the hiding property of the underlying commitment scheme (cf. §
2.5.3
).
Before proceeding with the output reconstruction in the online phase, we need to ensure that all the honest parties are alive after the verification phase. For this, all the parties maintain an
aliveness
bit, say
\mathsf{b}
, which is initialized to
\mathtt{continue}
. If the verification phase is not successful for a party, it sets
\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{abort}
. In the first round of reconstruction, the parties broadcast their
\mathsf{b}
bit and accept the value that forms the majority. If
\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{continue}
, then a pair of parties open the commitment (communicated in the preprocessing) towards the third party. This method is fair because at least one honest party would have provided the correct opening to allow the third party to obtain its missing share. The formal protocol appears in Fig.
4.7
.

4.2.4
Achieving Robustness

To elevate the security of
SWIFT
to robustness, we use the robust variant of
\mathsf{jsnd}
in all the protocols. Moreover, for reconstruction, we use the fair reconstruction protocol in Fig.
4.7
except that the aliveness check (Online, Step 1) is no longer required. This is because the verification in robust
\mathsf{jsnd}
guarantees identification of a
\mathsf{TTP}
in case of any inconsistency, and the parties wouldn’t have executed the reconstruction protocol.

4.2.5
Multi-input Multiplication

3-input multiplication

To compute
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{abc}
, note that

\displaystyle\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{abc}-\mathsf{r}=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}})-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{abc}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ac}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bc}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}-\mathsf{r}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\footnotesize{(cf. notation\penalty\ \ref{notation:3pcMconcise})}}

(4.4)

Similar to

, for

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bc}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ac}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-{(\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}+\mathsf{r})}^{1}

\displaystyle P_{2},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bc}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ac}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-{(\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}+\mathsf{r})}^{2}

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bc}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ac}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-{(\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}+\mathsf{r})}^{3}

\mathsf{isTr}
