4.3
Garbled World

Similar to
ASTRA
, we have two variants –
SWIFT
T
requiring communication of 2 GCs and one online round, and
SWIFT
C
requiring 1 GC and two rounds.
The 2 GC variant has two parallel executions, each comprising of 2 garblers and 1 evaluator.
P_{1},P_{2}
act as evaluators in two independent executions and the parties in
\Phi_{1}=\{P_{2},P_{3}\}
,
\Phi_{2}=\{P_{1},P_{3}\}
act as garblers, respectively. The 1 GC variant comprises of a single execution with
\Phi_{1}
acting as garblers and
P_{1}
as the evaluator.

4.3.1
2 GC Variant

Input Phase

Here, the boolean values
(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3})
act as the
new
inputs for the garbled computation. The semantics of
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
-sharing ensures that each of these shares is available with at least two parties (including evalator) in each garbling instance. Thus, the goal of our input phase is to create the compound sharing,
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}=({\llbracket\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf G})
for every input
\mathsf{x}
to the function to be evaluated via the GC.
Consider the garbling instance with
P_{1}
as the evaluator. The number of input keys for this instance can be further reduced by treating
(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2})
as a single input. For the case of arithmetic values, the input changes to
(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2})
. Similarly, the other instance with
P_{2}
as evaluator uses
(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1})
as input.
We first discuss the semantics for
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-sharing followed by steps for generating
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
-sharing.

Garbled sharing semantics

A value
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-shared (garbled shared) amongst
\mathcal{P}
if
P_{3}
holds
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{i}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,2})
,
P_{1}
holds
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{1}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,2})
and
P_{2}
holds
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{2}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},2})
. Here,
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},j}=\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,j}\oplus\mathsf{v}\Delta^{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
, and
\Delta^{j}
, which is known only to the garblers in
\Phi_{j}
, denotes the global offset with its least significant bit set to
1
and is same for every wire in the circuit.
A value
\mathsf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is said to be
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
-shared (compound shared) if each value from
(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3})
is
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-shared. We write
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}=({\llbracket\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf G})
.

Generation of
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
and
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}

Protocol
\mathrm{\Pi}_{\mathsf{Sh}}^{\bf G}(\mathcal{P},\mathsf{v})
(Fig.
4.9
) enables generation of
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
where two garblers in each garbling instance hold
\mathsf{v}
, and proceeds as follows. Consider the first garbling instance with evaluator
P_{1}
and garblers
P_{s},P_{t}
. Garblers in
\Phi_{1}
generate
\{\mathsf{K}_{{{\mathsf{v}}}}^{\mathsf{b},1}\}_{\mathsf{b}\in\{0,1\}}
which denotes the key for value
\mathsf{b}
on wire
\mathsf{v}
, following the free-XOR technique
[
82
,
84
]
. If the value
\mathsf{v}
is known to both
P_{s},P_{t}
, they
\mathsf{jsnd}
the respective key to
P_{1}
. Else, w.l.o.g. let
P_{s}\in\Phi_{j}
be the garbler that knows
\mathsf{v}
. To ensure the correct key delivery towards
P_{1}
, we make garblers
P_{s},P_{t}
commit to both the keys to
P_{1}
via
\mathsf{jsnd}
.
P_{s}
then sends the opening for commitment of
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},j}
to
P_{1}
. If the decommitment fails,
P_{1}
abort for the case of secuirty with abort or fairness. For robustness it accuses
P_{s}
and
P_{t}
is chosen as the
\mathsf{TTP}
.

Similar steps carried out with respect to the second garbling instance, at the end of which, garblers in
\Phi_{2}
possess
\{\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{b},2}\}_{\mathsf{b}\in\{0,1\}}
while the evaluator
P_{2}
holds
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},2}
. Following this, the shares
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{s}
held by
P_{s}\in\mathcal{P}
are defined as
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{0}=(\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{0,2})
,
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{1}=(\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},1},\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{0,2})
,
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{2}=(\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},2})
.
To generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
,
\mathrm{\Pi}_{\mathsf{Sh}}^{\bf G}
is invoked for each of
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2}
, and
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}
.

\justify

Input(s):

\mathsf{v}
,
Output:

{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
.

Let
P_{s}\in\Phi_{j}
be the garbler that knows
\mathsf{v}
in clear where
j\in\{1,2\}
and
P_{t}
be the co-garbler in
\Phi_{j}
.

1.

Garblers in
\Phi_{j}
generate keys
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,j},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{1,j}
for wire
\mathsf{v}
, using free-XOR technique.

2.

If both
P_{s}
and
P_{t}
know
\mathsf{v}
in clear,
P_{s},P_{t}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},j}
to evaluator
P_{j}
.

3.

Else, parties proceed as follows:

(a)

P_{s},P_{t}
prepare commitment on both the keys as
\mathsf{Com}(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,j}),\mathsf{Com}(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{1,j})
and communicates to evaluator
P_{j}
in a random permuted order using
\mathsf{jsnd}
.

(b)

P_{s}
sends the opening for commitment of
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},j}
to
P_{j}
.

(c)

If the decommitment using the opening fails,
P_{j}

\mathtt{abort}
for the case of security with abort or fairness. For robustness,
P_{j}
broadcasts
“
(accuse,
P_{s}
)
”
and
P_{t}
is chosen as the
\mathsf{TTP}
.

4.

P_{0}
sets
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{0}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,2})
,
P_{1}
sets
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{1}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,2})
and
P_{2}
sets
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{2}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},2})
.

Figure 4.9
:
Generation of
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
in
SWIFT
.

Evaluation

Let
f(\mathsf{x})
be the function to be evaluated. At this point, the function input is
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
-shared. This renders
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-sharing for the input of the GC that corresponds to the function
f^{\prime}\big(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}\big)
which first combines the given boolean-shares to compute the actual input and then applies
f
on it. Let
\mathsf{GC}_{j}
denote the garbled circuit to be sent to
P_{j}\in\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
by garblers in
\Phi_{j}
. Sending of
\mathsf{GC}_{j}
is overlapped with the key transfer (during generation of
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
), to save rounds, where garblers
\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{GC}_{j}
to
P_{j}
. On receiving the
\mathsf{GC}
, evaluators evaluate their respective GCs and obtain the key corresponding to the output, say
\mathsf{z}
. This generates
{\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
.

Output phase

The goal of output computation is to compute the output
\mathsf{z}
from
{\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
.
To reconstruct
\mathsf{z}
towards
P_{j}\in\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
, garblers in
\Phi_{j}

\mathsf{jsnd}
the least significant bit
\mathsf{p}^{j}
of
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{z}}^{0,j}
, referred to as the decoding information, to
P_{j}
.
P_{j}
uses the received
\mathsf{p}^{j}
to reconstruct
\mathsf{z}
as
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{p}^{j}\oplus\mathsf{q}^{j}
, where
\mathsf{q}^{j}
denotes the least significant bit of
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\mathsf{z},j}
.
P_{1},P_{2}
then
\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{z}
to
P_{3}
completing the protocol. Reconstruction is lightweight and requires a single round towards
P_{3}
while reconstruction towards
P_{1},P_{2}
can be overlapped with key transfer and does not incur extra rounds. The protocol appears in Fig.
4.10
.

\justify

Input(s):

{\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
,
Output:

\mathsf{z}
.

1.

For an output wire
\mathsf{z}
, let
\mathsf{p}^{j}
denote the least significant bit of
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{0,j}
and
\mathsf{q}^{j}
denote the least significant bit of
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{\mathsf{z},j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
.

: Parties in
\Phi_{j}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{p}^{j}
to
P_{j}
who reconstructs
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{p}^{j}\oplus\mathsf{q}^{j}
.

:
P_{1},P_{2}

\mathsf{z}
to

.

:

in
SWIFT
.

Optimizations when deployed in mixed framework

Working in the preprocessing model enables transfer of the (communication-intensive) GC and generating
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-shares of the input-independent shares of
\mathsf{x}
(i.e.
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}
) in the preprocessing. Thus, the online phase is very light and only requires one round to generate
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-shares for the input-dependent data (i.e.
{\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}}
). Since evaluation is local, evaluators obtain
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-sharing of the GC output at the end of
1
round. Moreover, we require the garbled output to be reconstructed towards both
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
in clear. Thus, the steps for reconstruction towards
P_{3}
can be avoided in
\mathrm{\Pi}_{\mathsf{Rec}}^{\bf G}
protocol (Fig.
4.10
).

4.3.2
1 GC Variant

The garbling scheme here is similar to the 2GC variant except that now there exists only a single garbling instance. Parties in
\Phi_{1}=\{P_{2},P_{3}\}
act as the garblers while
P_{1}
act as the evaluator. Looking ahead, in the mixed protocol framework, the output has to be reconstructed towards
P_{1},P_{2}
. Reconstruction towards
P_{1}
does not incur additional rounds since sending of decoding information can be overlapped with the key transfer. To reconstruct towards
P_{2}
,
P_{1}
sends the least significant bit of
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{\mathsf{z},1}
, denoted by
\mathsf{q}^{1}
, along with a hash of
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{\mathsf{z},j}
to
P_{2}
. Party
P_{2}
accepts
\mathsf{q}^{1}
if the hash is consistent with the respective key. This is fine since a corrupt
P_{1}
cannot send an incorrect key due to the
authenticity
of the garbling scheme
[
15
]
. On the other hand, if the hash is inconsistent,
P_{2}
aborts for the case of security with abort or fairness. For robustness it accuses
P_{1}
and
P_{3}
is chosen as the
\mathsf{TTP}
.

4.4
Security proofs

Without loss of generality, we prove the security of our robust framework. The case for fairness follows similarly, and we omit its details. We provide proofs in the
\{\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{setup}},\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{MultPre}$}},\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{jsnd}$}}\}
-hybrid model, where
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{setup}}
(§
2.5.1
),
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{MultPre}$}}
(Fig.
4.5
) and
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{jsnd}$}}
(Fig.
5.18
) denote the ideal functionality for the shared-key setup, preprocessing of multiplication (
\Pi_{\mathsf{MultPre}}
) and
\mathsf{jsnd}
, respectively.

The strategy for simulating the computation of function
f
(represented by a circuit
\mathsf{Ckt}
) is as follows. The simulation begins with the simulator emulating the shared-key setup (
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{setup}}
) functionality and giving the respective keys to the adversary. This is followed by the input sharing phase in which
\mathcal{S}
computes the input of
\mathcal{A}
, using the known keys, and sets the honest parties’ inputs to be used in the simulation to
0
.
\mathcal{S}
invokes the ideal functionality
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{GOD}}
on behalf of
\mathcal{A}
using the extracted input and obtains the output
\mathsf{y}
.
\mathcal{S}
now knows the inputs of
\mathcal{A}
and can compute all the intermediate values for each building block.
\mathcal{S}
proceeds with simulating each of the building blocks in the topological order. We provide the simulation for the case for corrupt
P_{1}
and
P_{3}
. The case for corrupt
P_{2}
is similar to that of
P_{1}
.

For modularity, we provide the simulation steps for each building block separately. Carrying out these blocks in the topological order yields the simulation for the entire computation. If a
\mathsf{TTP}
is identified during the simulation, the simulator stops and returns the function output to the adversary on behalf of the
\mathsf{TTP}
as per
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{jsnd}$}}
.

Ideal
\mathsf{jsnd}
Functionality

The ideal
\mathsf{jsnd}
functionality for fairness security appears in Fig.
4.11
and that for the robust setting appears in Fig.
4.1
.

\justify

interacts with the parties in
\mathcal{P}
and the adversary
\mathcal{S}
.

Step 1:

\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{jsnd}$}}
receives
(\mathsf{Input},\mathsf{v}_{s})
from senders
P_{s}
for
s\in\{i,j\}
,
(\mathsf{Input},\bot)
from receiver
P_{k}
. While sending the inputs, the adversary is also allowed to send a special
\mathtt{abort}
command.

Step 2:

Set
\mathsf{msg}_{i}=\mathsf{msg}_{j}=\mathsf{msg}_{l}=\bot
.

Step 3:

If
\mathsf{v}_{i}=\mathsf{v}_{j}
, set
\mathsf{msg}_{k}=\mathsf{v}_{i}
. Else, set
\mathsf{msg}_{k}=\mathtt{abort}
.

Step 4:

Send
(\mathsf{Output},\mathsf{msg}_{s})
to
P_{s}
for
s\in\{1,2,3\}
.

\mathcal{A}

\mathcal{A}
are sampled using the respective keys, while others are sampled randomly. The details for the online phase are provided next. We omit the simulation for corrupt
P_{3}
as it is similar to that of
P_{1}
.

–

If dealer is
\mathcal{A}

\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}

\mathcal{A}

P_{2}

\mathcal{A}

\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{GOD}}
on input

to obtain the function output
\mathsf{y}
.

P_{2}
or
P_{3}

\mathcal{S}_{\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}}^{P_{1}}

\mathsf{v}=0
and performs the protocol steps honestly.

for corrupt

Shares unknown to
\mathcal{A}
are sampled randomly in the simulation, whereas in the real protocol, they are sampled using the pseudorandom function (PRF). The indistinguishability of the simulation thus follows by a reduction to the security of the PRF. The same holds for the rest of the blocks.

The simulation for the joint sharing protocol (
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
) is similar to that of the sharing protocol. The protocol’s design is such that the simulator will always know the value to be sent as part of the joint sharing protocol. The communication is constituted by
\mathsf{jsnd}
calls and is emulated according to the simulation of
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{jsnd}$}}

Reconstruction Protocol (

, Fig.

Using the input of
\mathcal{A}
obtained during simulation of sharing protocol,

on behalf of
\mathcal{A}
and obtains the function output
\mathsf{y}
in clear.

\mathcal{A}
using
\mathsf{y}
and the other shares. The missing share is then communicated to
\mathcal{A}
by emulating the
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{jsnd}$}}
functionality.

Chapter 5
Tetrad
: 4PC Fair and Robust Protocols

This chapter provides details for the Layer I blocks of our 4PC framework
Tetrad
.
Some of the results in this chapter resulted in publications at NDSS’20
[
38
]
and NDSS’22
[
87
]
.
Depending on the sensitivity of the application and the underlying data, we may want different levels of security. For this, we propose multiple variants of the framework, covering fairness (
Tetrad
) and robustness (
Tetrad-R
I
,
Tetrad-R
II
) guarantees. Comparison of
Tetrad
with actively secure 4PC PPML frameworks, in terms of the communication for multiplication, is presented in Table
5.1
.

†
†
nicematrix-placeholder:
NiceTabular (nicematrix)

Table 5.1
:
Comparison of malicious 4PC frameworks for PPML

5.1
Preliminaries and Definitions

We consider
4
parties denoted by
\mathcal{P}=\{P_{0},P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}\}
that are connected by pair-wise private and authentic channels in a synchronous network, and a static, active adversary that can corrupt at most 1 party.

5.1.1
Sharing Semantics

For the arithmetic and boolean sharing, we follow a
(4,1)
replicated secret sharing (RSS)
[
38
]
, where a value
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
is split into four shares. To leverage the benefits of the preprocessing paradigm, we associate meaning to the shares and demarcate the parties in terms of their roles. Three of the shares of a
(4,1)
RSS can be generated in the preprocessing phase independent of the value to be shared, and their sum can be interpreted as a mask. The fourth share, dependent on
\mathsf{v}
, can be computed in the online phase and can be treated as the masked value. We denote the three preprocessed shares as
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}
and the mask as
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}
. The masked value is denoted as
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
, and
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
.

Table 5.2
:
Sharing semantics for a value
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
in
Tetrad
.
†
†
nicematrix-placeholder:
NiceTabular (nicematrix)

Next, we distinguish the four parties into two sets; the
eval
set
\mathcal{E}=\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
which is assigned the task of carrying out the computation, and is active throughout the online phase. The
helper
set
\mathcal{D}=\{P_{0},P_{3}\}
, is used to assist
\mathcal{E}
in verification, and so it is only active towards the end of the computation. Complying with the roles and RSS format, the distribution is done as follows:
P_{0}:\{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}\},P_{1}:\{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}\},P_{2}:\{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}\}
, and
P_{3}:\{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}\}
.
The shares are distributed among
\mathcal{D}
such that
P_{3}
gets
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
whereas
P_{0}
gets all the shares of
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
. In the preprocessing phase,
P_{0}
computes a part of the data needed for verification (cf. Fig.
5.3
) using its input independent shares, which is communicated to
P_{3}
. This enables a verification in the online, without
P_{0}
, for the fair protocols.

The RSS sharing semantics is presented in Table
5.2
, denoted by
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
, in a modular way with the help of three intermediate sharing semantics
\left[\cdot\right],\left\lparen\hskip-5.4778pt\middle\lparen\mathopen{}\cdot\mathclose{}\middle\rparen\hskip-5.4778pt\right\rparen
and
\langle\cdot\rangle
. All the sharings used are linear i.e. given sharings of values
\mathsf{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{v}_{m}
and public constants
c_{1},\ldots,c_{m}
, sharing of
\sum_{i=1}^{m}c_{i}\mathsf{v}_{i}
can be computed non-interactively for an integer
m
.

Notation 5.1.1

(a) For the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of
n
values
\mathsf{a}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{a}_{n}
,
\gamma_{\mathsf{a}_{1}\ldots\mathsf{a}_{n}}=\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}
and
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{1}\ldots\mathsf{a}_{n}}{}=\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}{}
(b) We use superscripts
{\bf B}
, and
{\bf G}
to denote sharing semantics in boolean, and garbled world, respectively–
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
,
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
. We omit the superscript for arithmetic world.

Sharing semantics for boolean sharing over
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is similar to arithmetic sharing except that addition is replaced with XOR. The semantics for garbled sharing are described in §
5.3
with the relevant context.

5.1.1.1
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{zero}}
- Generating additive shares of zero

In
Tetrad
, we make use of a functionality
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{zero}}
to enable parties
P_{0},P_{i}
obtain
Z_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
such that
Z_{1}+Z_{2}+Z_{3}=0
. We observe that the functionality can be instantiated non-interactively using the pre-shared keys (cf. §
2.5.1
). For this, parties in
\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{j}\}
sample random value
\mathsf{r}_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
. The shares are then defined as
Z_{1}=\mathsf{r}_{3}-\mathsf{r}_{2},Z_{2}=\mathsf{r}_{1}-\mathsf{r}_{3}
and
Z_{3}=\mathsf{r}_{2}-\mathsf{r}_{1}
.

5.1.2
Joint-Send (
\mathsf{jsnd}
) Primitive

The Joint-Send (
\mathsf{jsnd}
) primitive, for the case of security with fairness, allows to parties
P_{i},P_{j}
to relay a message
\mathsf{v}
to a third party
P_{k}
ensuring either the delivery of the message or
\mathtt{abort}
in case of inconsistency. Towards this,
P_{i}
sends
\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
, while
P_{j}
sends a hash of the same (
\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{v})
) to
P_{k}
. Party
P_{k}
accepts the message if the hash values are consistent and
\mathtt{abort}
otherwise. Note that the communication of the hash can be clubbed together for several instances and be deferred to the end of the protocol, amortizing the cost.

Joint-Send (
\mathsf{jsnd}
) for robust protocols

The
\mathsf{jsnd}
primitive (Fig.
5.1
), for the case of robustness, allows two senders
P_{i},P_{j}
to relay a common message,
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
, to recipient
P_{k}
, either by ensuring successful delivery of
\mathsf{v}
, or by establishing a Trusted Third Party (
\mathsf{TTP}
) among the parties. The instantiation of
\mathsf{jsnd}
can be viewed as consisting of two phases (
send, verify
), where the
send
phase consists of
P_{i}
sending
\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
and the rest of the protocol steps go to
verify
phase (which ensures correct
send
or
\mathsf{TTP}
identification). This requires
1
round of interaction and
\ell
bits of communication. To leverage amortization,
verify
will be executed only once, at the end the computation, requiring
2
rounds.

Note that the appropriate instantiation of
\mathsf{jsnd}
is used depending on the security guarantee. For simplicity, protocols where the fair and robust variants only differ in the instantiation of
\mathsf{jsnd}
used, we give a common construction for both.

Notation 5.1.2

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}
denotes the instantiation of Joint-Send (
\mathsf{jsnd}
) primitive. We say that
P_{i},P_{j}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
when they invoke
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}(P_{i},P_{j},\mathsf{v},P_{k})
.

Input(s):

P_{i},P_{j}:\mathsf{v}
,
P_{k}:\bot
,
Output:

P_{i},P_{j}:\bot/\mathsf{TTP}
,
P_{k}:\mathsf{v}/\mathsf{TTP}
.

\justify
P_{s}\in\mathcal{P}
initializes an inconsistency bit
\mathsf{b}_{s}=0
. If
P_{s}
remains silent instead of sending
\mathsf{b}_{s}
in any of the following rounds, the recipient sets
\mathsf{b}_{s}
to
1
.

–

Send:

P_{i}
sends
\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
.

–

Verify:

–

P_{j}
sends
\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{v})
to
P_{k}
.

P_{k}
sets
\mathsf{b}_{k}=1
if the received values are inconsistent or if the value is not received.

–

P_{k}
sends
\mathsf{b}_{k}
to all parties.
P_{s}
for
s\in\{i,j,l\}
sets
\mathsf{b}_{s}=\mathsf{b}_{k}
.

–

P_{s}
for
s\in\{i,j,l\}
mutually exchange their bits.
P_{s}
resets
\mathsf{b}_{s}=\mathsf{b}^{\prime}
where
\mathsf{b}^{\prime}
denotes the bit which appears in majority among
\mathsf{b}_{i},\mathsf{b}_{j},\mathsf{b}_{l}
.

–

All parties set
\mathsf{TTP}=P_{l}
if
\mathsf{b}^{\prime}=1
, terminate otherwise.

Figure 5.1
:
Joint-Send for robust protocols in
Tetrad
.

Lemma 5.1.3

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}
(Fig.
5.1
) requires an amortized communication of
\ell
bits and
1
round.

Proof 5.1.4
.

In the protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}(P_{i},P_{j},\mathsf{v},P_{k})
for the fair variant,
P_{i}
communicates
\mathsf{v}
to
P_{k}
requiring communication of
\ell
bits and one round. The hash value communication from
P_{j}
to
P_{k}
can be clubbed for multiple instances with the same set of parties and hence the cost gets amortized. The analysis is similar for the robust case as well. Here, though the verification consists of multiple steps, the cost gets amortized over multiple instances.

5.2
Arithmetic / Boolean 4PC

This section covers the details of our 4PC protocol
Tetrad
over an arithmetic ring
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
. We begin by explaining the sharing protocol in §
5.2.1
, multiplication with abort in §
5.2.2
, and the reconstruction in §
5.2.3
. Lastly, the details on elevating the security to fairness are presented in §
5.2.3.1
and to robustness in §
5.2.4
.

5.2.1
Sharing

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
(Fig.
5.2
) enables
P_{i}
to generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-share of a value
\mathsf{v}
. During the preprocessing phase,
\mathsf{\lambda}
-shares are sampled non-interactively using the pre-shared keys (cf. §
2.5.1
) in a way that
P_{i}
will get the entire mask
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
. During the online phase,
P_{i}
computes
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
and sends to
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
, which exchange the hash values to check for consistency. Parties abort in the fair protocol in case of inconsistency, whereas for robust security, parties proceed with a default value.

Input(s):

P_{i}:\mathsf{v}
,
Output:

\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

Sample as follows:

P_{i},P_{0},P_{1},P_{3}:\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{i},P_{0},P_{2},P_{3}:\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{i},P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}

\justify

Online:

1.

P_{i}
computes
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
and sends to
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
.

2.

P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
mutually exchange
\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}})
and accept the sharing if there exists a majority. Else parties
\mathtt{abort}
for the case of fairness and accepts a default value for the case of robust security.

Figure 5.2
:
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of a value
\mathsf{v}
by party
P_{i}
in
Tetrad
.

Lemma 5.2.5

(Communication)
.

Protocol

(Fig.

3\ell
bits and
1
round in the online phase.

Proof 5.2.6
.

The preprocessing of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
is non-interactive as the parties sample non interactively using key setup
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{Setup}}
(§
2.5.1
). in the online phase,
P_{i}
sends
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
to
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
resulting in 1 round and communication of at most
3\ell
bits (
P_{i}=P_{0}
). The next round of hash exchange can be clubbed for several instances and the cost gets amortized over multiple instances.

5.2.1.1
Joint Sharing

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
enables parties

to generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-share of a value
\mathsf{v}
. The protocol is similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
except that
P_{j}
ensures the correctness of the sharing performed by
P_{i}
. During the preprocessing,
\mathsf{\lambda}
-shares are sampled such that both
P_{i},P_{j}
will get the entire mask
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
. During the online phase,
P_{i},P_{j}
compute and
\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
to parties
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
.

For joint-sharing a value
\mathsf{v}
possessed by
P_{0}
along with another party in the preprocessing, the communication can be optimized further. The protocol steps based on the
(P_{i},P_{j})
pair are summarised below:

–

(P_{0},P_{1}):\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{2}\}
sample
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
; Parties set
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=0
;
P_{0},P_{1}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}=-\mathsf{v}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}
to
P_{2}
.

–

(P_{0},P_{2}):\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{3}\}
sample
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
; Parties set
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=0
;
P_{0},P_{2}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}=-\mathsf{v}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}
to
P_{3}
.

–

(P_{0},P_{3}):\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{1}\}
sample
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
; Parties set
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}=0
;
P_{0},P_{3}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}=-\mathsf{v}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}
to
P_{1}
.

5.2.2
Multiplication

Given the shares of
\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b}
, the goal of the multiplication protocol is to generate shares of
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{ab}
. The protocol is designed such that parties
P_{1},P_{2}
obtain a masked version of the output
\mathsf{z}
, say
\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}
in the online phase, and
P_{0},P_{3}
obtain the mask
\mathsf{r}
in the preprocessing phase. Parties then generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of these values by executing
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
, and locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{r}\rrbracket
to obtain the final output.

Online

Note that,

\displaystyle\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}-\mathsf{r}=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}})-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{r}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{\footnotesize{(cf. notation\penalty\ \ref{notation:4pcconcise})}}

(5.1)

In Eq

,
P_{1},P_{2}
can compute
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}
locally, and hence we are interested in computing

and
P_{2}

P_{1},P_{2}

\displaystyle P_{1}:\mathsf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{r}\right]_{1}

\displaystyle P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{r}\right]_{2}

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}

(5.2)

The preprocessing is set up such that

receive an additive sharing (

\mathsf{isTr}

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{2}:\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{2}

.

Verification

To ensure the correctness of the values exchanged, we use the assistance of
P_{3}
. Concretely,
P_{3}
obtains
\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{s}
, where
\mathsf{s}
is a random mask known to
P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
. For this
P_{3}
needs
\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}+\mathsf{s}
, which it obtains from the preprocessing phase. The mask
\mathsf{s}
is used to prevent the leakage from
\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}
to
P_{3}
.
P_{3}
computes a hash of
\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{s}
and sends it to
P_{1},P_{2}
, which
\mathtt{abort}
if it is inconsistent.

Preprocessing

Parties should obtain the following values from the preprocessing phase:

{\sf i)}\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{1},P_{2}:\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-\mathsf{r}\right]\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \penalty\ {\sf ii)}\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{0},P_{3}:\mathsf{r}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \penalty\ {\sf iii)}\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{3}:\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}+\mathsf{s}

For
{\sf i)}
and
{\sf ii)}
, let
\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}=\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{1}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{2}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{3}
, where
P_{0}
along with
P_{i}
can compute
\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{i}
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. For
P_{1},P_{2}
, to form an additive sharing of
(\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}-r)
, it suffices for them to define their share as
\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{i}+\left[\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{3}-r\right]
.
Instead of sampling a random
\mathsf{r}
,
P_{0},P_{3}
, along with
P_{i}
, sample the share for
\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{3}-\mathsf{r}
as
\mathsf{u}^{i}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
.
P_{0},P_{3}
compute
\mathsf{r}
as
\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{3}-\mathsf{u}^{1}-\mathsf{u}^{2}
.

For
{\sf iii)}
,
P_{3}
needs
\mathsf{w}=\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{1}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{2}+\mathsf{s}
. To tackle this,
P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
sample
\mathsf{s}_{1},\mathsf{s}_{2}
, and set
\mathsf{s}=\mathsf{s}_{1}+\mathsf{s}_{2}
.
P_{0},P_{i}
, for
i\in\{1,2\}
,
\mathsf{jsnd}

\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{i}+\mathsf{s}_{i}
to
P_{3}
. This requires a communication of 2 elements.
As an optimization,
P_{0}
sends
\mathsf{w}
to
P_{3}
. If
P_{0}
is malicious, it might send a wrong value to
P_{3}
. However, in this case, every party in the online phase would be honest. And since
P_{1},P_{2}
do not use
\mathsf{w}
in their computation, any error in
\mathsf{w}
is bound to get caught in the verification phase.

Lemma 5.2.7

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
(Fig.
5.3
) (in
Tetrad
) requires
2\ell
bits of communication in the preprocessing phase, and
1
round and
3\ell
bits of communication in the online phase.

Proof 5.2.8
.

During preprocessing, sampling of values
{\mathsf{u}}^{1},{\mathsf{u}}^{2}
are performed non-interactively using
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{Setup}}
. A communication of
\ell
bits is required for the joint sharing of
\mathsf{q}
by
P_{0},P_{3}
as explained in §
5.2.1.1
. In addition,
P_{0}
communicates
\mathsf{w}
to
P_{3}
requiring additional
\ell
bits.
During online, two instances of
\Pi_{\mathsf{jsnd}}
are executed in parallel resulting in a communication of
2\ell
bits and 1 round. This is followed by a joint sharing by
P_{1},P_{2}
for which an additional communication of
\ell
bits are required. However, in joint sharing, the communication is from
P_{1}
to
P_{3}
and the same can be deferred till the verification stage. Thus the online round is retained as
1
in an amortized sense.

5.2.2.1
Truncation

To accommodate truncation, the multiplication protocol is modified as follows.
P_{1},P_{2}
locally truncate
(\mathsf{z-r})
and generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of it in the online phase. Similarly,
P_{0},P_{3}
truncate
\mathsf{r}
in the preprocessing phase and generate its
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares. Parties locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket=\llbracket(\mathsf{z-r})^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket
.

5.2.2.2
Multiplication with constant

Multiplication by a constant in MPC is typically local. Given constant
\alpha
and
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
, the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of the product
\mathsf{y}=\alpha\mathsf{v}
can be locally computed as per (
5.3
).

\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}}=\alpha\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{u}},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{1}=\alpha\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{2}=\alpha\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2},\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{3}=\alpha\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}

(5.3)

However, in FPA, we need to perform a truncation on the output. For this, note that the product can be written as
\alpha\mathsf{v}=\beta^{1}+\beta^{2}
where
\beta^{1}=\alpha.(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3})
and
\beta^{2}=\alpha.(-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2})
.

P_{1},P_{2}
locally truncate
\beta^{1}
and execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
, while
P_{0},P_{3}
do the same with
\beta^{2}
.

\langle\cdot\rangle

\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b}

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{1}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \mathsf{jsnd}\penalty\ \mathsf{(ab)}^{1}=\mathsf{a}^{1}\mathsf{b}^{3}+\mathsf{a}^{3}\mathsf{b}^{1}+\mathsf{a}^{3}\mathsf{b}^{3}+Z_{1}\penalty\ \text{to }P_{2}.

5.2.3

.

.

The protocol involves 4 invocations of

.

5.2.3.1
Achieving Fairness

Here, we show how to extend the security of
Tetrad
from abort to fairness. Before proceeding with the output reconstruction, we must ensure that all the honest parties are alive after the verification phase. For this, all the parties maintain an
aliveness
bit, say
\mathsf{b}
, which is initialized to
\mathtt{continue}
. If the verification phase is not successful for a party, it sets
\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{abort}
. In the first round of reconstruction, the parties mutually exchange their
\mathsf{b}
bit and accept the value that forms the majority. Since we have only one corruption, it is guaranteed that all the honest parties will agree on
\mathsf{b}
. If
\mathsf{b}=\mathtt{continue}
, the parties exchange their missing shares and accept the majority. As per the sharing semantics, every missing share is possessed by three parties, out of which there can be at most one corruption. As an optimization, for instances where many values are reconstructed, two out of the three parties can send the share while the third can send a hash of the same.

Looking ahead, a similar reconstruction will be used for the robust variants as well. However, there is no need to perform an explicit aliveness check as it will be taken care of by the verification of
\mathsf{jsnd}
instances.

5.2.4
Achieving Robustness

In this section, we show how to extend the security of
Tetrad
to robustness. We provide two variants with different trade-offs in the communication for multiplication – i)
Tetrad-R
I
: It has the same amortized communication complexity as that of
Tetrad
but requires verification in the preprocessing phase, and ii)
Tetrad-R
II
: It avoids the verification in
Tetrad-R
I
but incurs a communication overhead of
1
element in the preprocessing phase over
Tetrad
.

5.2.4.1
Tetrad-R
I

On a high level, we make two modifications to the multiplication protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
(Fig.
5.3
). In the preprocessing, communication comes from a
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
in step 3 of the protocol, and the value
\mathsf{w}
sent by
P_{0}
to
P_{3}
, in step 4. To get robustness, the robust variant of
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
is used. To ensure the correctness of
\mathsf{w}
, we introduce
\Pi_{\mathsf{VrfyP0}}
(Fig.
5.7
). If
\Pi_{\mathsf{VrfyP0}}
fails, parties identify a
\mathsf{TTP}
in the preprocessing phase itself. The second modification is in the online phase, which proceeds as that of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
. If any
\mathtt{abort}
happens,
P_{0}
is assigned as the
\mathsf{TTP}
. Since
P_{0}
does not participate in the online phase of the multiplication, and its communication in the preprocessing has been verified via
\Pi_{\mathsf{VrfyP0}}
, this assignment is safe.

Verifying the communication by
P_{0}
:

In
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
(Fig.
5.3
) protocol,
P_{0}
computes and sends
\mathsf{w}=\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{1}+\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{2}+{\mathsf{s}}_{1}+{\mathsf{s}}_{2}
to
P_{3}
with
P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
knowing
{\mathsf{s}}_{1},{\mathsf{s}}_{2}
in clear. Note that
\mathsf{w}=\mathsf{w}^{1}+\mathsf{w}^{2}
for
\mathsf{w}^{1}=\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{1}+{\mathsf{s}}_{1}
and
\mathsf{w}^{2}=\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{2}+{\mathsf{s}}_{2}
. Also,
P_{0}
along with
P_{1},P_{2}
and
P_{3}
possess the values
\mathsf{w}^{1},\mathsf{w}^{2}
and
\mathsf{w}
respectively. Checking the correctness of
\mathsf{w}
reduces to verifying
\mathsf{w}=\mathsf{w}^{1}+\mathsf{w}^{2}
.

To verify this relation for all
M
multiplication gates in the circuit, i.e.
\{\mathsf{w}_{j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}\mathsf{w}_{j}^{1}+\mathsf{w}_{j}^{2}\}_{j\in[M]}
, one approach is to compute a random linear combination and verify the relation on the sum.
While working over a field
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, this solution has an error probability
1/|\mathbb{F}_{p}|
, where
|\mathbb{F}_{p}|
denotes the size of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
. However, this solution does not work naively over rings since not every element in the ring has an inverse, unlike fields. Concretely, the check can still pass with a probability of at most
1/2

[
1
,
27
]
. To reduce the cheating probability, the check is repeated
\kappa
times, thereby bounding the cheating probability by
1/2^{\kappa}
. As an optimization, it is sufficient to choose the random combiners from
\{0,1\}
. Thus, parties need to sample only a binary string of
M
bits using the shared key for one check. The formal verification protocol appears in Fig.
5.6
.

Input(s):

P_{0},P_{1}:{\mathsf{w}}_{j}^{1}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{0},P_{2}:{\mathsf{w}}_{j}^{2}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{0},P_{3}:{\mathsf{w}}_{j}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\
, for
j=1,\ldots,M
.

Output:
Whether
{\mathsf{w}}_{j}={\mathsf{w}}_{j}^{1}+{\mathsf{w}}_{j}^{2}
or not, for
j=1,\ldots,M
.

\justify
Repeat the following
\kappa
times, in parallel.

1.

Sample random values
\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{M}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

2.

Locally compute:
P_{0},P_{1}:\mathsf{e}^{1}=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\tau_{j}\mathsf{w}_{j}^{1}
;
P_{0},P_{2}:\mathsf{e}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\tau_{j}\mathsf{w}_{j}^{2}
;
P_{0},P_{3}:\mathsf{e}=\sum_{j=1}^{M}\tau_{j}\mathsf{w}_{j}
.

3.

(P_{0},P_{1})
,
(P_{0},P_{2})
and
(P_{0},P_{3})
generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares of
\mathsf{e}^{1},\mathsf{e}^{2}
and
\mathsf{e}
respectively using
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
.

4.

Locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{g}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{e}\rrbracket-\llbracket\mathsf{e}^{1}\rrbracket-\llbracket\mathsf{e}^{2}\rrbracket
.

5.

Robustly reconstruct
\mathsf{g}
and check if
\mathsf{g}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}0
.

If for all
\kappa
repetitions,
\mathsf{g}=0
, then continue with rest of the computation. Else,
P_{0}
is identified to be corrupt and
\mathsf{TTP}=P_{1}
.

Figure 5.6
:
Verifying
P_{0}
’s communication in the multiplication protocol of
Tetrad-R
I
: Approach 1

Another approach, that avoids the repetition in the
\Pi_{\mathsf{VrfyP0}}
protocol above, is to perform a similar check over a Galois ring
[
1
,
27
]
. To carry out the verification, the extended ring
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}/f(x)
is used, which is the ring of all polynomials with coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
modulo an irreducible polynomial
f
of degree
d
over
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Here, each element in
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
is lifted to a
d
-degree polynomial in
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}[x]/f(x)
(which results in blowing up the communication by a factor
d
). Given this, to verify the
M
values, further packing is performed. More concretely, assume that
d
divides
M
and
M=d\cdot q
. For
j=1,\ldots,q
, public polynomial
g_{j}
and shared polynomials
g^{1}_{j}
and
g^{2}_{j}
are defined for each set of
d
values
\{\mathsf{w},\mathsf{w}^{1},\mathsf{w}^{2}\}
, all of which are then combined to check whether
\{\mathsf{w}_{j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}\mathsf{w}_{j}^{1}+\mathsf{w}_{j}^{2}\}_{j\in[M]}
. We describe the polynomial with respect to
j=1
below.

\displaystyle g_{1}=\mathsf{w}_{1}+X\cdot\mathsf{w}_{2}+\ldots+X^{d-1}\cdot\mathsf{w}_{d}

\displaystyle g_{1}^{1}=\mathsf{w}^{1}_{1}+X\cdot\mathsf{w}^{1}_{2}+\ldots+X^{d-1}\cdot\mathsf{w}^{1}_{d}

\displaystyle g_{1}^{2}=\mathsf{w}^{2}_{1}+X\cdot\mathsf{w}^{2}_{2}+\ldots+X^{d-1}\cdot\mathsf{w}^{2}_{d}

Now, parties sample random values
\mathsf{r}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{r}_{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}/f(x)
and compute
g=\sum_{j=1}^{q}\mathsf{r}_{j}g_{j}
,
g^{1}=\sum_{j=1}^{q}\mathsf{r}_{j}g^{1}_{j}
and
g^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{q}\mathsf{r}_{j}g^{2}_{j}
. This is followed by robustly reconstructing
g-g^{1}-g^{2}
and verifying if this value is
0
. If not,
P_{0}
is identified to be a corrupt and computation is carried out by a
\mathsf{TTP}
. The formal verification protocol appears in Fig.
5.7
.

Input(s):

P_{0},P_{1}:{\mathsf{w}}_{j}^{1}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{0},P_{2}:{\mathsf{w}}_{j}^{2}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{0},P_{3}:{\mathsf{w}}_{j}\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\
, for
j=1,\ldots,M
.

Output:
Whether
{\mathsf{w}}_{j}={\mathsf{w}}_{j}^{1}+{\mathsf{w}}_{j}^{2}
or not, for
j=1,\ldots,M
.

\justify

1.

Define the following polynomials over
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}/f(x)
for
j\in[q]
.

\displaystyle g_{j}=\mathsf{w}_{1+(j-1)d}+X\cdot\mathsf{w}_{2+(j-1)d}+\ldots+X^{d-1}\cdot\mathsf{w}_{d+(j-1)d}

\displaystyle g^{1}_{j}=\mathsf{w}^{1}_{1+(j-1)d}+X\cdot\mathsf{w}^{1}_{2+(j-1)d}+\ldots+X^{d-1}\cdot\mathsf{w}^{1}_{d+(j-1)d}

\displaystyle g^{2}_{j}=\mathsf{w}^{2}_{1+(j-1)d}+X\cdot\mathsf{w}^{2}_{2+(j-1)d}+\ldots+X^{d-1}\cdot\mathsf{w}^{2}_{d+(j-1)d}

2.

Parties generate random values
\mathsf{r}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{r}_{q}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}/f(x)
, and compute
g=\sum_{j=1}^{q}\mathsf{r}_{j}g_{j}
,
g^{1}=\sum_{j=1}^{q}\mathsf{r}_{j}g^{1}_{j}
and
g^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{q}\mathsf{r}_{j}g^{2}_{j}
.

3.

Parties execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}(P_{0},P_{1},g^{1})
,
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}(P_{0},P_{2},g^{2})
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}(P_{0},P_{3},g)
to generate
\llbracket g^{1}\rrbracket,\llbracket g^{2}\rrbracket
and
\llbracket g\rrbracket
, respectively.

4.

Parties robustly reconstruct
g-g^{1}-g^{2}
and check equality to
0
. If it is
0
, then parties continue with rest of the computation. Else,
P_{0}
is identified to be corrupt and
\mathsf{TTP}=P_{1}
.

Figure 5.7
:
Verifying
P_{0}
’s communication in the multiplication protocol of
Tetrad-R
I
: Approach 2

5.2.4.2
Tetrad-R
II

This variant (Fig.
5.8
) avoids the verification of
P_{0}
at the cost of communicating 1 extra ring element in the preprocessing, compared to
Tetrad-R
I
. Note that the communication cost of this protocol is similar to that of the one in SWIFT
[
85
]
. We were unable to extend the latter’s efficiently to support multi-input multiplication.
Hence, we design
Tetrad-R
II
that has the same communication complexity as SWIFT but also supports multi-input multiplication, as well as truncation without any overhead. In order to get rid of
\Pi_{\mathsf{VrfyP0}}
, the communication of
\mathsf{w}
from
P_{0}
to
P_{3}
is split into 2 parts. (
P_{0},P_{1}
) and (
P_{0},P_{2}
) compute
\mathsf{w}
in parts, and send them to
P_{3}
using
\mathsf{jsnd}
.
This modification allows
P_{3}
to compute
\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{s}_{1}
and
\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{s}_{2}
separately in the online phase. In addition, to enable
P_{2}
to obtain
\mathsf{y}_{1}
,
P_{1},P_{3}
can
\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{s}_{1}
to
P_{2}
.
P_{1}
obtains
\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{s}_{2}
similarly.

The formal protocol for the robust multiplication in
Tetrad-R
II
,
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}^{\mathsf{R}}
, appears in Fig.
5.8
. The primary difference from the fair counterpart is that the communication of
\mathsf{w}
from
P_{0}
to
P_{3}
in the preprocessing is now split into two parts.
(P_{0},P_{1}),(P_{0},P_{2})
communicates
\mathsf{w}_{1},\mathsf{w}_{2}
respectively to
P_{3}
via
\mathsf{jsnd}
.

\mathsf{isTr}
is a bit denoting whether truncation is required (
\mathsf{isTr}=1
) or not (
\mathsf{isTr}=0
).

Input(s):

\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
.

Output:

\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket
where
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=0
and
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{ab}
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

1.

Parties locally compute the following:

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{1}:\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{1}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{3}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{3}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3}

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{2}:\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{3}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{3}:\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{3}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}

2.

P_{0},P_{3}
and
P_{j}
sample random
{\mathsf{u}}^{j}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
. Let
{\mathsf{u}^{1}}+\mathsf{u}^{2}=\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{3}-\mathsf{r}
for a random
\mathsf{r}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

3.

P_{0},P_{3}
compute
\mathsf{r}=\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{3}-{\mathsf{u}^{1}}-\mathsf{u}^{2}
and set
\mathsf{q}=\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
, else set
\mathsf{q}=\mathsf{r}
.
P_{0},P_{3}
execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}(P_{0},P_{3},\mathsf{q})
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket
.

4.

P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
sample random
{\mathsf{s}}_{1},{\mathsf{s}}_{2}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.
P_{0},P_{j}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{w}_{j}=\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}^{j}+{\mathsf{s}}_{j}
to
P_{3}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
.

\justify

Online:

Let
\mathsf{y}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}+\mathsf{s}_{1}+\mathsf{s}_{2}
.

1.

Parties locally compute the following:

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{s}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+{\mathsf{u}}^{1}+{\mathsf{w}}_{1}

\displaystyle P_{2},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{s}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}+{\mathsf{u}}^{2}+{\mathsf{w}}_{2}

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}

2.

P_{1},P_{3}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{s}_{1}
to
P_{2}
, while
P_{1},P_{3}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{s}_{2}
to
P_{1}
.

3.

P_{1},P_{2}
locally compute
\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}=(\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{y}_{3})+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{s}_{1}-\mathsf{s}_{2}
.

4.

If
\mathsf{isTr}=1
,
P_{1},P_{2}
locally set
\mathsf{p}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})^{\mathsf{t}}
, else
\mathsf{p}=\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}
.
P_{1},P_{2}
execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}(P_{1},P_{2},\mathsf{p})
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket
.

5.

Parties locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket
. Here
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{z}
otherwise.

Figure 5.8
:
Robust multiplication in
Tetrad-R
II
.

Lemma 5.2.11

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}^{\mathsf{R}}
(Fig.
5.8
) (in
Tetrad-R
II
) requires
3\ell
bits of communication in the preprocessing phase, and
1
round and
3\ell
bits of communication in the online phase.

Proof 5.2.12
.

During preprocessing, the sampling of values
{\mathsf{u}}^{1},{\mathsf{u}}^{2}
are performed non-interactively using
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{Setup}}
. A communication of
\ell
bits is required for the joint sharing of
\mathsf{q}
by
P_{0},P_{3}
as explained in §
5.2.1.1
. In addition,
P_{0},P_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
communicates
\mathsf{w}_{j}
to
P_{3}
via
\mathsf{jsnd}
requiring additional
2\ell
bits.
The online phase is similar to the fair multiplication protocol (
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
) and the costs follow from Lemma
5.2.7
.

5.2.5
Multi-input Multiplication

The goal of 3-input multiplication is to generate
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}\mathsf{c}
given
\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{c}\rrbracket
. For this parties proceed similar to the multiplication protocol (see §
5.2.2
), where they compute
\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{r}\rrbracket
. Observe that

\displaystyle\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}\mathsf{c}-\mathsf{r}=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}})-\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}\mathsf{c}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}\mathsf{c}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}\gamma_{\mathsf{b}\mathsf{c}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{c}}+\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}}-\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{b}\mathsf{c}}-\mathsf{r}

Similar to the 2-input fair multiplication
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
(Fig.
5.3
), the goal of the preprocessing phase is to generate additive shares of
\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}},\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}},\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}},\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}+\mathsf{r}
among
P_{1},P_{2}
.

Informally, the terms that
P_{1},P_{2}
cannot compute locally for the aforementioned
\gamma
values, can be computed by
P_{0},P_{3}
, as evident from our sharing semantics.
P_{0},P_{3}
compute the missing terms and share them among
P_{1},P_{2}
in the preprocessing phase.
P_{1},P_{2}
proceed with online phase similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
, to compute
\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}
. Thus the online complexity is retained as that of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
while the preprocessing communication is increased to 9 elements. The protocol appears in Fig.
5.9
.

\mathsf{isTr}
is a bit denoting whether truncation is required (
\mathsf{isTr}=1
) or not (
\mathsf{isTr}=0
).

Input(s):

\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{c}\rrbracket
.

Output:

\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket
where
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=0
and
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{abc}
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

1.

Computation for
\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}
: Invoke
\Pi_{\mathsf{MultS}}
(Fig.
5.4
) on
\langle\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{a}}\rangle
and
\langle\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}\rangle
to generate
\langle\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}\rangle
.

2.

Computation for
\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}
:

–

Parties locally compute the following:

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{1}:\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}^{1}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{3}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{3}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{1}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{3}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{3}

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{2}:\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{3}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{3}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{2}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{2}

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{3}:\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}^{3}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{2}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{1}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{1}

–

P_{0},P_{3}
and
P_{1}
sample random
{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{ac}}^{1}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.
P_{0},P_{3}
compute and
\mathsf{jsnd}

{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{ac}}^{2}=\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}^{3}-{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{ac}}^{1}
to
P_{2}
.

–

P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
sample random
{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathsf{ac}}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.
P_{0}
sends
{\mathsf{w}}_{\mathsf{ac}}=\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}^{1}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}^{2}+{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathsf{ac}}
to
P_{3}
.

3.

Computation for
\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}
: Similar to Step 2 (for
\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}
).
P_{1},P_{2}
obtain
{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{bc}}^{1},{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{bc}}^{2}
respectively such that
{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{bc}}^{1}+{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{bc}}^{2}=\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}^{3}
.
P_{3}
obtains
{\mathsf{w}}_{\mathsf{bc}}=\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}^{1}+\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}^{2}+{\mathsf{s}}_{\mathsf{bc}}
.

4.

Computation for
\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}
:

–

Using
\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}
(Step 1),
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}
, compute the following:

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{1}:\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{1}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{3}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{3}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{1}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{3}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{3}

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{2}:\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{2}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{3}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{3}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{2}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{2}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{2}

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{3}:\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}^{3}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{1}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{2}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{2}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{1}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{1}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{1}

–

P_{0},P_{3}
and
P_{j}
sample random
{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{abc}}^{j}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
. Let
{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1}+{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{abc}}^{2}=\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}^{3}+\mathsf{r}
for
\mathsf{r}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

–

P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
sample random
{\mathsf{s}}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.
P_{0}
sends
{\mathsf{w}}_{\mathsf{abc}}=\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1}+\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}^{2}+{\mathsf{s}}
to
P_{3}
.

5.

P_{0},P_{3}
compute
\mathsf{r}={\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{abc}}^{1}+{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{abc}}^{2}-\gamma_{\mathsf{abc}}^{3}
and set
\mathsf{q}=\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
, else set
\mathsf{q}=\mathsf{r}
. Execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}(P_{0},P_{3},\mathsf{q})
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket
.

\justify

Online:

Let
\mathsf{y}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{abc}}
.

1.

Parties locally compute the following:

\displaystyle P_{1}:\mathsf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bc}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ac}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}+(\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}^{1}+{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{ac}}^{1})\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}+(\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}^{1}+{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{bc}}^{1})\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-(\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{bc}}^{1}+{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{bc}}^{1})

\displaystyle P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{2}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bc}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ac}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}+(\gamma_{\mathsf{ac}}^{2}+{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{ac}}^{2})\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}-(\gamma_{\mathsf{bc}}^{2}+{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{bc}}^{2})\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}}-(\gamma_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{bc}}^{2}+{\mathsf{u}}_{\mathsf{a}\mathsf{bc}}^{2})

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{3}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{bc}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ac}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{c}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{ab}}+\gamma_{\mathsf{ab}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{c}}

2.

P_{1}
sends
\mathsf{y}_{2}
to
P_{2}

5.2.6
Supporting on-demand computations

For on-demand applications where the underlying function to be computed is not known in advance, the preprocessing model is not desirable. We observe that the
Tetrad
protocol can be modified by executing the preprocessing phase in the online phase itself, keeping the same overall communication cost. The formal protocol appears in Fig.
5.11
.

We provide the fair multiplication,
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}^{\mathsf{NoPre}}
, for
function-independent
preprocessing in Fig.
5.11
. The protocol incurs no overhead over the fair multiplication (
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
) in
Tetrad
. This is due to the design of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
where values
{\mathsf{u}}^{1},{\mathsf{u}}^{2}
are sampled non-interactively in the preprocessing. Thus the joint-sharing by
P_{0},P_{3}
(Step 5 (a) in Fig.
5.11
) can be performed along with the communication among
P_{1},P_{2}
(Step 4 in Fig.
5.11
) in the online. Moreover, the rest of the communication can be deferred till the verification stage and thus, the online round complexity is retained. The protocol for robust setting is similar.

\mathsf{isTr}
is a bit denoting whether truncation is required (
\mathsf{isTr}=1
) or not (
\mathsf{isTr}=0
).

if

if

5.2.6.1
Comparison with Fantastic Four
[
46
]

We analyse the performance of Fantastic Four
[
46
]
where execution proceeds in segments (cf. §6.4,
[
46
]
). Elaborately, computation is carried out optimistically for each segment, followed by a verification phase before proceeding to the next segment. If verification fails, the current segment is recomputed via an active 3PC protocol. Subsequent segments also proceed with a 3PC execution until the verification fails again. In this case, a semi-honest 2PC with a helper is carried out for the current and rest of the segments. For analysis, we consider their best and worst-case execution cost.

Table 5.3
:
Comparison with Fantastic Four
[
46
]
†
†
nicematrix-placeholder:
NiceTabular (nicematrix)

Observe that the best case happens when the verification is always successful, which we call as
Case I
. In this case, the communication cost is that of the 4PC execution. Note that an adversary can
always
make the verification fail in the first segment itself. This results in executing the entire protocol (all segments) with their active 3PC, which accounts for their worst-case cost. We denote this as
Case II
. Their 3PC protocols are designed to work over the extended ring of size
\ell+\kappa
bits. As evident from Tables 2, 3 of their paper, their 3PC is at least
10\times
more expensive than their 4PC in terms of both runtime and communication. Thus, the higher cost of 3PC defeats the purpose of having an additional honest party in the system.

Observe that their protocols are designed to work with a function-independent preprocessing. Thus, for a fair comparison, we compare both cases against the on-demand variants of our robust protocols (
Tetrad-R
I
,
Tetrad-R
II
). The results are summarised in Table
5.3
. We remark that the values for their cases are obtained from Table 1 of their paper
[
46
]
.

5.3
Garbled World

In the applications we consider, the garbled circuit is used as an intermediary to evaluate certain functions where the input to the function as well as the output are in
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shared (or
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
-shared) form.

Instantiating the garbled world using existing 4PC GC-based protocols
[
72
,
30
]
turn out to be overkill, as they are standalone protocols. For instance,
[
72
]
provides robust protocols by communicating
12
GCs while
[
30
]
requires generating and exchanging commitments on the inputs to ensure input consistency. On the other hand, the inputs to our protocol are consistent (due to
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing), and we do not need an explicit reconstruction, making it lighter overall.

Towards this, we propose 2 GC protocols –
Tetrad
T
requiring communication of 2 GC evaluations and 1 online round, and
Tetrad
C
requiring 1 GC and 2 rounds. Moreover, these protocols leverage the benefit of amortization which comes from using
\mathsf{jsnd}
.
The 2 GC variant has two parallel executions, each comprising of 3 garblers and 1 evaluator.
P_{1},P_{2}
act as evaluators in two independent executions and the parties in
\Phi_{1}=\{P_{0},P_{2},P_{3}\}
,
\Phi_{2}=\{P_{0},P_{1},P_{3}\}
act as garblers, respectively. The 1 GC variant comprises of a single execution with
\Phi_{1}
acting as garblers and
P_{1}
as the evaluator.
Leveraging an honest majority among the garblers and using
\mathsf{jsnd}
, we only need semi-honest GC computation to get active security.

5.3.1
2 GC Variant

Input Phase.

Given that the function input
\mathsf{x}
is already available as
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
, the boolean values
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}
, where
\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}}=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2}
and
\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}\oplus\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}
, act as the
new
inputs for the garbled computation, and garbled sharing (
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
) is generated for each of these values. The semantics of
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
-sharing ensures that each of these shares (
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}
) is available with two garblers in each garbling instance. The keys for the shares can either be sent (using
\mathsf{jsnd}
) correctly to the evaluators or the inconsistency is detected. This key delivery essentially generates
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-sharing for each of these three values which enables GC evaluation. Thus, the goal of our input phase is to create the compound sharing,
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}=({\llbracket\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}\rrbracket}^{\bf G})
for every input
\mathsf{x}
to the function to be evaluated via the GC. We first discuss the semantics for
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-sharing followed by steps for generating
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
-sharing.

Garbled sharing semantics.

A value
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-shared (garbled shared) amongst
\mathcal{P}
if
P_{i}\in\{P_{0},P_{3}\}
holds
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{i}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,2})
,
P_{1}
holds
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{1}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,2})
and
P_{2}
holds
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{2}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},2})
. Here,
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},j}=\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,j}\oplus\mathsf{v}\Delta^{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
, and
\Delta^{j}
, which is known only to the garblers in
\Phi_{j}
, denotes the global offset with its least significant bit set to
1
and is same for every wire in the circuit.
A value
\mathsf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is said to be
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
-shared (compound shared) if each value from
(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3})
, which are as defined above, is
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-shared. We write
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}=({\llbracket\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}\rrbracket}^{\bf G})
.

Generation of
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
and
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}

Protocol
\mathrm{\Pi}_{\mathsf{Sh}}^{\bf G}(\mathcal{P},\mathsf{v})
(Fig.
5.12
) enables generation of
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
where two garblers in each garbling instance hold
\mathsf{v}
, and proceeds as follows. Consider the first garbling instance with evaluator
P_{1}
where garblers
P_{k},P_{l}
hold
\mathsf{v}
. Garblers in
\Phi_{1}
generate
\{\mathsf{K}_{{{\mathsf{v}}}}^{\mathsf{b},1}\}_{\mathsf{b}\in\{0,1\}}
which denotes the key for value
\mathsf{b}
on wire
\mathsf{v}
, following the free-XOR technique
[
82
,
84
]
.

P_{k},P_{l}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{K}_{{{\mathsf{v}}}}^{\mathsf{v},1}
to evaluator
P_{1}
. Similar steps carried out with respect to the second garbling instance, at the end of which, garblers in
\Phi_{2}
possess
\{\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{b},2}\}_{\mathsf{b}\in\{0,1\}}
while the evaluator
P_{2}
holds
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},2}
. Following this, the shares
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{s}
held by
P_{s}\in\mathcal{P}
are defined as
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{0}={\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{3}=(\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{0,2})
,
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{1}=(\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},1},\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{0,2})
,
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{2}=(\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},2})
.

\justify

1.

Garblers in
\Phi_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
generate keys
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,j},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{1,j}
for wire
\mathsf{v}
, using free-XOR technique.

2.

Let
P_{k}^{j},P_{l}^{j}
denote the garblers in the
j^{\text{th}}
garbling instance, for
j\in\{1,2\}
, who hold
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.
P_{k}^{j},P_{l}^{j}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v},j}
to evaluator
P_{j}
.

3.

P_{i}\in\{P_{0},P_{3}\}
sets
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{i}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,2})
,
P_{1}
sets
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{1}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,2})
and
P_{2}
sets
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}_{2}=(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0,1},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{\mathsf{v},2})
.

Figure 5.12
:
Generation of
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}

To generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
, we need a way to generate
({\llbracket\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}\rrbracket}^{\bf G})
, given
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
. For this,
\mathrm{\Pi}_{\mathsf{Sh}}^{\bf G}
is invoked for each of
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}
.

Evaluation.

Let
f(\mathsf{x})
be the function to be evaluated. At this point, the function input is
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
-shared. This renders
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-sharing for the input of the GC that corresponds to the function
f^{\prime}\big({\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}},{\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}}},{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}}\big)
which first combines the given boolean-shares to compute the actual input and then applies
f
on it. Let
\mathsf{GC}_{j}
denote the garbled circuit to be sent to
P_{j}\in\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
by garblers in
\Phi_{j}
. Sending of
\mathsf{GC}_{j}
is overlapped with the key transfer (during generation of
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
), to save rounds, where garblers in
\{P_{0},P_{3}\}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{GC}_{j}
to
P_{j}
. On receiving the
\mathsf{GC}
, evaluators evaluate their respective GCs and obtain the key corresponding to the output, say
\mathsf{z}
. This generates
{\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
.

Output phase.

The goal of output computation is to compute the output
\mathsf{z}
from
{\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
.
To reconstruct
\mathsf{z}
towards
P_{j}\in\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
, two garblers in
\Phi_{j}
send the least significant bit
\mathsf{p}^{j}
of
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{z}}^{0,j}
, referred to as the decoding information, to
P_{j}
. If the received values are consistent,
P_{j}
uses the received
\mathsf{p}^{j}
to reconstruct
\mathsf{z}
as
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{p}^{j}\oplus\mathsf{q}^{j}
, where
\mathsf{q}^{j}
denotes the least significant bit of
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\mathsf{z},j}
; else
P_{j}
aborts.
To reconstruct
\mathsf{z}
towards the garblers
P_{g}\in\{P_{0},P_{3}\}
, one evaluator, say
P_{1}
sends the least significant bit,
\mathsf{q}^{1}
, of
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{\mathsf{z},1}
along with
\mathcal{H}=\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{\mathsf{z},1})
to
P_{g}
, where
\mathsf{H}
is a collision-resistant hash function. If a garbler received a consistent
(\mathsf{q}^{1},\mathcal{H})
pair from
P_{1}
such that there exists a
K\in\{\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{{0,1}},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{{1,1}}\}
whose least significant bit is
\mathsf{q}^{1}
and
\mathsf{H}(K)=\mathcal{H}
, then it uses
\mathsf{q}^{1}
for reconstructing
\mathsf{z}
; else the garbler aborts the computation.
Note that a corrupt evaluator
P_{1}
cannot create confusion among garblers in
\{P_{0},P_{3}\}
by sending the key that was not output by the GC owing to the authenticity of the garbling scheme. Reconstruction is lightweight and requires a single round for garblers while reconstruction towards evaluators can be overlapped with key transfer and does not incur extra rounds.
The protocol appears in Fig.
5.13
.

-

For an output wire
\mathsf{z}

\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{0,j}
and
\mathsf{q}^{j}
denote the least significant bit of
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{\mathsf{z},j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
.

-

Reconstruction towards
P_{j}\in\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
: Garblers

in
\Phi_{j}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{p}^{j}
to
P_{j}
. If
P_{j}
received consistent values from
P_{0},P_{3}
, it reconstructs
\mathsf{z}
as
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{p}^{j}\oplus\mathsf{q}^{j}
.

-

Reconstruction towards
P_{g}\in\{P_{0},P_{3}\}
:
P_{1}
sends
\mathsf{q}^{1}
and
\mathcal{H}=\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{\mathsf{z},1})
to
P_{g}
, where
\mathsf{H}
is a collision-resistant hash function.
P_{g}
uses the
\mathsf{q}^{1}
received from
P_{1}
for reconstructing
\mathsf{z}
as
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{p}^{1}\oplus\mathsf{q}^{1}
if there exists a
K\in\{\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{{0,1}},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{{1,1}}\}
whose least significant bit is
\mathsf{q}^{1}
and
\mathsf{H}(K)=\mathcal{H}
.

:
Output computation: reconstruction of
\mathsf{z}

Optimizations when deployed in mixed framework.

Working in the preprocessing model enables transfer of the (communication-intensive) GC and generating
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-shares of the input-independent shares of
\mathsf{x}
(i.e.
{\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}}},{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}}
) in the preprocessing phase. Thus, the online phase is very light and only requires one round to generate
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-shares for the input-dependent data (i.e.
{\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}}
). Since evaluation is local, evaluators obtain
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-sharing of the GC output at the end of
1
round.

Achieving fairness and robustness.

To ensure fairness, we require a fair reconstruction protocol that proceeds as follows. As described in §
5.2.3.1
, parties first ensure that all parties are alive.
If so, they proceed similar to the protocol in Fig.
5.13
, except with the following differences. For reconstruction towards evaluators,
all
three respective garblers send it the decoding information. The evaluator selects the value appearing in the majority for reconstruction. For reconstruction towards garblers
P_{0},P_{3}
,
both
the evaluators send the least significant bit of the output key together with its hash to the garbler. The presence of at least one honest evaluator guarantees that both garblers will be on the same page.
The protocol appears in Fig.
5.14
.

\justify

-

Parties perform a bit exchange as described in §
5.2.3.1
to ensure that all parties are alive. If all parties are alive, they proceed as follows.

-

For an output wire
\mathsf{z}
, let
\mathsf{p}^{j}
denote the least significant bit of
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{0,j}
and
\mathsf{q}^{j}
denote the least significant bit of
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{\mathsf{z},j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
.

-

Reconstruction towards
P_{j}\in\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
: Garblers in
\Phi_{j}
send
\mathsf{p}^{j}
to
P_{j}
.
P_{j}
selects the value forming majority among these and reconstructs
\mathsf{z}
as
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{p}^{j}\oplus\mathsf{q}^{j}
.

-

Reconstruction towards
P_{g}\in\{P_{0},P_{3}\}
:
P_{j}\in\{P_{1},P_{2}\}
sends
\mathsf{q}^{j}
and
\mathcal{H}^{j}=\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{\mathsf{z},j})
to
P_{g}

\mathsf{H}
is a collision-resistant hash function.
P_{g}
uses the
\mathsf{q}^{1}
received from
P_{1}
for reconstructing
\mathsf{z}
as
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{p}^{1}\oplus\mathsf{q}^{1}
if there exists a

whose least significant bit is
\mathsf{q}^{1}
and
\mathsf{H}(K)=\mathcal{H}^{1}
. Else, it computes
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{p}^{2}\oplus\mathsf{q}^{2}
.

Figure 5.14
:

\mathsf{z}

The main difference from its fair counterpart is the use of a robust
\mathsf{jsnd}
primitive to achieve robustness. This guarantees that a
\mathsf{TTP}
is identified if misbehaviour is detected, taking the computation to completion and delivering the output to all.

5.3.2
1 GC Variant

The input
\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{x}_{1}\oplus\mathsf{x}_{2}
for this variant consists of two shares,
\mathsf{x}_{1}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2}
and
\mathsf{x}_{2}=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}
, where
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}
are as defined in
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
. To ensure correct key transfer for the value
\mathsf{x}_{2}
held by garbler
P_{0}
and evaluator
P_{1}
, garblers
P_{0},P_{3}
commit to both keys for
\mathsf{x}_{2}
towards
P_{1}
, while
P_{0}
sends the opening to the key for
\mathsf{x}_{2}
. Then,
P_{1}
verifies the consistency of the received commitments and the opening, as it possesses
\mathsf{x}_{2}
. The protocol appears in Fig.
5.15
.

\justify

1.

\Phi_{1}
generate keys
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{0},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{v}}}^{1}
using free-XOR technique.

2.

If
(P_{i},P_{j})=(P_{2},P_{3})

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{v}}^{\mathsf{v}}

The evaluation and output phases are similar to the 2GC variant, except there is only a single garbling instance now. Looking ahead, in the mixed protocol framework, the output has to be reconstructed towards
P_{1},P_{2}
. Reconstruction towards
P_{1}
does not incur additional rounds since sending of decoding information can be overlapped with the key transfer. However, unlike in the 2GC variant where reconstruction towards
P_{2}
can be done similar to reconstruction towards
P_{1}
, in the 1GC variant, an additional round is required as
P_{2}
is no longer an evaluator. This incurs one extra round as opposed to the 2GC variant.

Achieving fairness.

To ensure fair reconstruction, as in §
5.2.3.1
, parties first perform an aliveness check. Following this, they proceed towards a fair reconstruction of
\mathsf{z}
from
{\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
as follows.
First, reconstruction of
\mathsf{z}
is carried out towards the garblers
P_{g}\in\Phi_{1}
. For this,
P_{1}
sends
\mathsf{q}
(least significant bit of
\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{\mathsf{z}}
) and
\mathcal{H}=\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{\mathsf{z}})
to
P_{g}
as before. Now, if a garbler received a consistent
(\mathsf{q},\mathcal{H})
pair from
P_{1}
such that there exists a
K\in\{\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{{0}},\mathsf{K}_{{\mathsf{z}}}^{{1}}\}
whose least significant bit is
\mathsf{q}
and
\mathsf{H}(K)=\mathcal{H}
, then it uses
\mathsf{q}
for reconstructing
\mathsf{z}
, and sends
\mathsf{z}
to its co-garblers. Else, a garbler accepts a
\mathsf{z}
received from a co-garbler as the output. Thus, further dissemination of the output by garblers ensures that all parties are on the same page.
If garblers receive the output, reconstruction of
\mathsf{z}
is carried out towards
P_{1}
. For this, all garblers (who received the output) send the decoding information to
P_{1}
, who selects the majority value to reconstruct
\mathsf{z}
.

\justify

-

Parties perform a bit exchange as described in §
5.2.3.1
to ensure that all parties are alive. If all parties are alive, they proceed as follows.

-

Let
\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q}
denote the least significant bit of
\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{z}}^{0},\mathsf{K}_{\mathsf{z}}^{\mathsf{z}}
, respectively.

-

Reconstruction towards garblers
P_{g}\in\Phi_{1}

\mathsf{q}
and

to

\mathsf{H}
is a collision-resistant hash function.
P_{g}

\mathsf{z}
.

P_{g}
