Proof 7.1.3
.

During preprocessing, generation of
\left[\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}\right]
involves communication of
\ell
bits from
P_{0}
to
P_{2}
. The online phase involves two instances of arithmetic sharing protocol in parallel, resulting in
1
round and a communication of
2\ell
bits.

7.1.3.1
Bit to Arithmetic:II

Similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
protocol, given the boolean sharings
{\llbracket\mathsf{b}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B},{\llbracket\mathsf{b}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{dbit2A}}
computes the arithmetic sharing of
(\mathsf{b}_{1}\mathsf{b}_{2})^{\sf R}
. Let
\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}
,
\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}
denote the value
(1-2\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R})
,
(1-2\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R})
respectively. Using (
7.1
), we can write

\displaystyle(\mathsf{b}_{1}\mathsf{b}_{2})^{\sf R}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}})^{\sf R}(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})^{\sf R}=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}+(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})^{\sf R}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}

(7.2)

During preprocessing, the
\left[\cdot\right]
-shares of
\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}
,
\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}
and
(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})^{\sf R}
are computed similar to that of
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
(Fig.
7.2
). The online phase is similar to that of
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
protocol.

Lemma 7.1.4

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{dbit2A}}
requires
3\ell
bits of communication in preprocessing, and
1
round and
2\ell
bits of communication in the online phase.

7.1.4
Bit Injection

Given the boolean sharing of a bit
\mathsf{b}
, denoted as
{\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
, and the arithmetic sharing of
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitInj}}
computes
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing of
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}\mathsf{v}
. Let
\Delta_{\mathsf{b}}
denote the value
(1-2\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R})
.
Similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}\mathsf{v}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}})^{\sf R}(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{})=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}}

(7.3)

During the preprocessing,
P_{0}
generates the
\left[\cdot\right]
-shares of
\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}
and
\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{}
similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
protocol. During the online phase,
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
compute an additive sharing of
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}\mathsf{v}
and execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
on them to generate the respctive
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares.

Lemma 7.1.5

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitInj}}
requires
2\ell
bits of communication in preprocessing, and
1
round and
2\ell
bits of communication in the online phase.

7.1.4.1
Sum of Bit Injections

Given
m
pair of values in the shared form,
\{{\llbracket\mathsf{b}_{i}\rrbracket}^{\bf B},\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{i}\rrbracket\}_{i\in[m]}
, the goal of
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitInjS}}
is to compute the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-share of
\mathsf{z}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathsf{b}_{i}^{\sf R}\cdot\mathsf{v_{i}}
. For this, parties execute the preprocessing corresponding to
m
bit injections of the form
\mathsf{b}_{i}^{\sf R}\cdot\mathsf{v_{i}}
.

In the online phase, each of
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
locally compute an additive sharing of
\mathsf{z}_{i}
, corresponding to
\mathsf{b}_{i}^{\sf R}\cdot\mathsf{v_{i}}
first. Instead of generating the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing for each of the
m
terms, parties locally add the shares and execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
on the result. Concretely, parties locally compute
\mathsf{z}^{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathsf{z}_{i}^{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
and execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
on
\mathsf{z}^{j}
to obtain its
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing. This results in an online communication independent of
m
.

Lemma 7.1.6

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitInjS}}
requires
m\cdot 2\ell
bits of communication in preprocessing, and
1
round and
2\ell
bits of communication in the online phase.

7.1.4.2
Bit Injection:II

Similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitInj}}
protocol, given
{\llbracket\mathsf{b}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B},{\llbracket\mathsf{b}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
and
\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{dbit2A}}
computes the arithmetic sharing of
(\mathsf{b}_{1}\mathsf{b}_{2})^{\sf R}\mathsf{v}
. Let
\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}
,
\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}
denote the value
(1-2\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R})
,
(1-2\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R})
respectively. Using (
7.1.3.1
) and (
7.1.4
), we can write

\displaystyle(\mathsf{b}_{1}\mathsf{b}_{2})^{\sf R}\mathsf{v}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}})^{\sf R}(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})^{\sf R}(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{})

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}+(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})^{\sf R}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ -\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}-\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}-\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}-(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})^{\sf R}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}

(7.4)

During the preprocessing,
P_{0}
generates the
\left[\cdot\right]
-shares of
\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}
,
\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}
,
\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
,
\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
,
(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})^{\sf R}
and
(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})^{\sf R}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
protocol. The online phase is similar to that of
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitInj}}
protocol.

Lemma 7.1.7

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{dbitInj}}
requires
6\ell
bits of communication in preprocessing, and
1
round and
2\ell
bits of communication in the online phase.

7.1.5
Equality Test (
\Pi_{\mathsf{eq}}
)

To check whether
\mathsf{a}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}\mathsf{b}
or not, given
\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
,
\Pi_{\mathsf{eq}}
proceeds with parties locally computing
\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket-\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
. According to our sharing semantics,
\mathsf{y}
can be written as
\mathsf{y}=\mathsf{y}_{1}-\mathsf{y}_{2}
where
\mathsf{y}_{1}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}}
and
\mathsf{y}_{2}=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}
.
P_{0}
generates
{\llbracket\mathsf{y}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
during the preprocessing while
P_{1},P_{2}
generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{y}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
in the online using
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
. Note that
\mathsf{a}=\mathsf{b}
implies
\mathsf{y}_{1}=\mathsf{y}_{2}
and hence all the bits of
\mathsf{v}=\overline{(\mathsf{y}_{1}\oplus\mathsf{y}_{2})}
should be
1
. As mentioned in the introduction of Part II (
II
), parties use four input AND gates and a tree structure, where
4
bits are taken at a time and the AND of them is computed in one go.

7.2
Mixed Protocol Framework

Table
7.1
compares our sharing conversions with ABY3
[
101
]
. For uniformity, we consider a function,
F
, to be computed on an
\ell
-bit inputs
\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}
using a garbled circuit (GC) in the mixed framework, which gives an
\ell
-bit output
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{F(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})}
, where
\ell
denotes the ring size in bits. Let
\mathsf{G}^{\sf F}
denote the corresponding GC. In the table,
\mathsf{G}^{\sf Sn}
denotes a
{\sf n}
-input garbled subtraction circuit;
\mathsf{G}^{\sf An}
denotes
{\sf n}
-input garbled addition circuit;
\hat{\mathsf{G}}
denotes the garbled circuit with decoding information;
\mathsf{G}^{\sf n_{1}\times 1,\ldots,n_{m}\times m}
denotes
{\sf n_{i}}
instances of GC
\mathsf{G}^{\sf i}
for
i\in\{1,\dots,{\sf m}\}
and
|\mathsf{G}^{\sf n_{1}\times 1,\ldots,n_{m}\times m}|
denotes its size.

:

[

]
and

.

7.2.1
Conversions involving Garbled World

Assume the GC is required to compute a function
f
on inputs
\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
and let the output be
f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})
. All the conversions described are for the 2 GC variant. Conversions for the 1 GC variant are straightforward, hence we omit the details.

Case I: Boolean-Garbled-Boolean

Since the inputs to the GC are available in boolean form, say
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf B},{\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
, parties generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C},{\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
by invoking the garbled sharing protocol
\mathrm{\Pi}_{\mathsf{Sh}}^{\bf G}
.
Additionally,
P_{0}
samples
\mathsf{R}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
to mask the function output,
f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})
, and generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{R}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
and
{\llbracket\mathsf{R}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
. Garblers
P_{g}\in\{P_{0},P_{2}\}
garble the circuit which computes
\mathsf{z}=f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})\oplus\mathsf{R}
, and send the GC along with the decoding information to evaluator
P_{1}
. Analogous steps are performed for evaluator
P_{2}
. Upon GC evaluation and output decoding, evaluators obtain
\mathsf{z}=f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})\oplus\mathsf{R}
, and jointly boolean share
\mathsf{z}
to generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
. Parties then compute
{\llbracket f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})\rrbracket}^{\bf B}={\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}\oplus{\llbracket\mathsf{R}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
.

Case II: Boolean-Garbled-Arithmetic

This is similar to
Case I
except that the circuit which computes
\mathsf{z}=f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})+\mathsf{R}
is garbled instead. Boolean sharing of
\mathsf{z}
is replaced with arithmetic, followed by computing
\llbracket f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket-\llbracket\mathsf{R}\rrbracket
.

Cases III & IV: Input in Arithmetic Sharing

The function to be computed
f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})
, is modified as
f^{\prime}(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}})=f(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}})
where inputs
\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}
are replaced by the pairs
\{\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}\},\{\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}\}
. The circuit to be garbled thus, corresponds to the function
f^{\prime}
. Parties generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G},{\llbracket\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
via
\mathrm{\Pi}_{\mathsf{Sh}}^{\bf G}
, following which, parties proceed with the rest of the computation whose steps are similar to
Case I
, and
II
, depending on the requirement on the output sharing.

7.2.2
Other Conversions

Arithmetic to Boolean

To convert arithmetic sharing of
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
to boolean sharing, observe that
\mathsf{v}=\mathsf{v}_{1}+\mathsf{v}_{2}
where
\mathsf{v}_{1}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
is possessed by parties
P_{1},P_{2}
, while
\mathsf{v}_{2}=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
is possessed by
P_{0}
. Thus,
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
can be computed as
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}={\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}+{\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
. For this,
P_{0}
can generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
in the preprocessing, and
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
can be generated in the online by
P_{1},P_{2}
using joint sharing protocol. The protocol appears in Fig.
7.3
. Boolean addition, when instantiated using the adder of ABY2.0
[
113
]
, requires
\log_{4}(\ell)
rounds.

\justify

Preprocessing:

P_{0}
generates
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
using
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
, where
\mathsf{v}_{2}=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
.

\justify

Online:

1.

P_{1},P_{2}
execute joint boolean sharing to generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
, where
\mathsf{v}_{1}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
.

2.

Parties obtain
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}={\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}+{\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
using a boolean adder circuit.

Figure 7.3
:
Arithmetic to Boolean Conversion in
ASTRA
.

Boolean to Arithmetic

To convert a boolean sharing of
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
into an arithmetic sharing, note that

\displaystyle\mathsf{v}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\mathsf{v}[i]=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}\oplus\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}[i]})=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\left(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}(1-2\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}^{\sf R})\right)

where
\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{v}[i]},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}^{\sf R}
denote the arithmetic value of bits
{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}},{\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}}
over the ring
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.
For each bit
\mathsf{v}[i]
of
\mathsf{v}
,
P_{0}
generates the
\left[\cdot\right]
-shares of
{{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}}}^{\sf R}
in the preprocessing, similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
(Fig.
7.2
). During the online phase, additive shares for each bit
\mathsf{v}[i]
are locally computed similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
. Parties then multiply the
i
th share with
2^{i}
and locally add up to obtain an additive sharing of
\mathsf{v}
. The rest of the steps are similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
, and the formal protocol appears in Fig.
7.4
.

Let
\mathsf{v}[i]
denote the
i
th bit of
\mathsf{v}
. Let
\mathsf{p}_{i}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}^{\sf R}
, and
\mathsf{q}_{i}=\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

1.

For
i\in\{0,1,\ldots,\ell-1\}
, execute the preprocessing of
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
(Fig.
7.2
) for each bit
\mathsf{v}[i]
, to generate
\left[{\mathsf{q}_{i}}\right]=(\left[\mathsf{q}_{i}\right]_{1},\left[\mathsf{q}_{i}\right]_{2})
.

\justify

Online:

Let
\mathsf{y}_{i}=(\mathsf{v}[i])^{\sf R}
and
\mathsf{y}
denotes the arithmetic equivalent of
\mathsf{v}
.

1.

Locally compute:

\displaystyle P_{1}:\mathsf{y}^{1}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\mathsf{y}_{i}^{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}(\mathsf{p}_{i}+\left[\mathsf{q}_{i}\right]_{1}(1-2\mathsf{p}_{i}))

\displaystyle P_{2}:\mathsf{y}^{2}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\mathsf{y}_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}(\left[\mathsf{q}_{i}\right]_{2}(1-2\mathsf{p}_{i}))

2.

P_{j}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
executes
\Pi_{\mathsf{Sh}}
on
\mathsf{y}^{j}
to generate the respective
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares.

3.

Compute
\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{y}^{1}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{y}^{2}\rrbracket
.

Figure 7.4
:
Boolean to Arithmetic Conversion in
ASTRA
.

Chapter 8
SWIFT
: 3PC Fair and Robust Blocks

This chapter provides details for the Layer II blocks of our 2PC framework
SWIFT
. Details for the Layer I blocks are provided in chapter
4
. The robust constructions of the blocks are detailed in this chapter, and the fair variants can be derived easily.

8.1
Building Blocks

8.1.1
Dot Product (Scalar Product)

Given
\llbracket\vec{\mathbf{a}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\vec{\mathbf{b}}\rrbracket
with
|\vec{\mathbf{a}}|=|\vec{\mathbf{b}}|=\mathsf{d}
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp}}
(Fig.
8.1
) computes
\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket
such that
\mathsf{z}=(\vec{\mathbf{a}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{b}})^{\mathsf{t}}
if truncation is enabled, else
\mathsf{z}=\vec{\mathbf{a}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{b}}
. For this, we combine the partial products from the multiplication protocol across
\mathsf{d}
multiplications and communicate them in a single shot. This makes the communication cost of the dot product independent of the vector size.

\mathsf{isTr}
is a bit denoting whether truncation is required (
\mathsf{isTr}=1
) or not (
\mathsf{isTr}=0
).

Input(s):

\llbracket\vec{\mathbf{a}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\vec{\mathbf{b}}\rrbracket
.

Output:

\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket
where
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=0
and
\mathsf{z}=\vec{\mathbf{a}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{b}}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}{\mathsf{a}}_{i}{\mathsf{b}}_{i}
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

Let
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}}=\{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}\}_{i\in[\mathsf{d}]}
,
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\vec{\mathbf{b}}}=\{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}\}_{i\in[\mathsf{d}]}
and
\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}\gamma_{\mathsf{a}_{i}\mathsf{b}_{i}}
.

1.

Invoke
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{dotpPre}$}}
on
\langle\mathsf{\lambda}_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}}\rangle
and
\langle\mathsf{\lambda}_{\vec{\mathbf{b}}}\rangle
to obtain
\langle\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}\rangle
.

2.

If
\mathsf{isTr}=0
:

(a)

Local computation of
\langle\mathsf{r}\rangle
:
\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{2}\}\leftarrow_{R}{\mathsf{r}}^{1}
;
\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{1}\}\leftarrow_{R}{\mathsf{r}}^{2}
;
\mathcal{P}\setminus\{P_{3}\}\leftarrow_{R}{\mathsf{r}}^{3}
.

(b)

Local computation of
\llbracket\mathsf{r}\rrbracket
:
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{r}}^{1}=-{\mathsf{r}}^{1}
,
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{r}}^{2}=-{\mathsf{r}}^{2}
,
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{r}}^{3}=-{\mathsf{r}}^{3}
,
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{r}}=0
. Set
\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{r}\rrbracket
.

3.

If
\mathsf{isTr}=1
, invoke
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
(Fig.
8.4
) to generate
(\langle\mathsf{r}\rangle,\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket)
. Set
\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket
.

4.

Locally compute
\langle(\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}-\mathsf{r})\rangle=\langle\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}\rangle-\langle\mathsf{r}\rangle
.

\justify

Online:

Let
\mathsf{y}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}\mathsf{b}_{i}}
.

1.

Parties locally compute the following:

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}})+{(\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}-\mathsf{r})}^{1}

\displaystyle P_{2},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}})+{(\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}-\mathsf{r})}^{2}

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}})+{(\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}-\mathsf{r})}^{3}

2.

P_{1},P_{3}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{y}_{1}
to
P_{2}
, while
P_{2},P_{3}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{y}_{2}
to
P_{1}
. They locally compute
\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}=(\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{y}_{3})+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}\mathsf{b}_{i}}
.

3.

P_{1},P_{2}
: If
\mathsf{isTr}=1
, set
\mathsf{p}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})^{\mathsf{t}}
, else
\mathsf{p}=\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}
. Execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}(P_{1},P_{2},\mathsf{p})
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket
.

4.

Compute
\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket
. Here
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{z}
otherwise.

Figure 8.1
:
Dot Product with / without Truncation in
SWIFT
.

Analogous to the multiplication protocol, dot product offloads one call to a robust dot product protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{MultPre}}
to the preprocessing. By extending techniques of
[
24
,
27
]
, we give an instantiation for the dot product protocol used in our preprocessing whose (amortized) communication cost is constant, thereby making our preprocessing cost also
independent
of
\mathsf{d}
. The ideal world functionality
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{dotpPre}$}}
for realizing
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}
is presented in Fig.
8.2
.

Instantiating
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{dotpPre}$}}
:

A trivial way to instantiate
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}
is to treat a dot product operation as
\mathsf{d}
multiplications. However, this results in a communication cost that is linearly dependent on the feature size. Instead, we instantiate
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}
by a semi-honest dot product protocol followed by a verification phase to check the correctness. For the verification phase, we extend the techniques of
[
24
,
27
]
to provide support for verification of dot product tuples. Setting the verification phase parameters appropriately gives a
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}
whose (amortized) communication cost is independent of the feature size. We will provide the details next.

\justify

\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{dotpPre}$}}
interacts with the parties in
\mathcal{P}
and the adversary
\mathcal{S}
.
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{dotpPre}$}}
receives
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of
\vec{\mathbf{u}}=\{\mathsf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{u}_{\mathsf{d}}\}
,
\vec{\mathbf{v}}=\{\mathsf{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{v}_{\mathsf{d}}\}
from the parties. Let
P^{\star}
denotes the party corrupted by
\mathcal{S}
.
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{dotpPre}$}}
receives
(\mathsf{w}_{i},\mathsf{w}_{j})
from
\mathcal{S}
as its share for
\langle\mathsf{w}\rangle
where
\mathsf{w}=\vec{\mathbf{u}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{v}}
.
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{dotpPre}$}}
proceeds as follows:

1.

Reconstructs
\vec{\mathbf{u}},\vec{\mathbf{v}}
using the shares received from honest parties and compute
\mathsf{w}=\vec{\mathbf{u}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{v}}
.

2.

Computes the third share
\mathsf{w}_{k}=\mathsf{w}-\mathsf{w}_{i}-\mathsf{w}_{j}
and sets
\langle\mathsf{w}\rangle_{1}=(\mathsf{w}_{1},\mathsf{w}_{3}),\langle\mathsf{w}\rangle_{2}=(\mathsf{w}_{2},\mathsf{w}_{3}),\langle\mathsf{w}\rangle_{3}=(\mathsf{w}_{1},\mathsf{w}_{2})
.

3.

Send
(\mathsf{Output},\langle\mathsf{w}\rangle_{s})
to
P_{s}\in\mathcal{P}
.

Figure 8.2
:
Ideal functionality for
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}
in
SWIFT
.

To realize
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{dotpPre}$}}
, the approach is to run a semi-honest dot product protocol followed by a verification phase to check the correctness of the output.
For verification, the work of
[
24
]
provides techniques to verify the correctness of
m
multiplication triples (and degree-two relations) at the cost of
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{m})
extended ring elements, albeit with abort security. While
[
27
]
improves their techniques to provide
robust
verification for multiplication, we show how to extend the techniques in
[
27
]
to robustly verify the correctness of
m
dot product tuples (dot product being a degree two relation), with vectors of dimension
\mathsf{d}
, at a cost of
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\mathsf{d}m})
extended ring elements. Thus, the cost to realize one instance of
\mathcal{F}_{\textsc{$\mathsf{dotpPre}$}}
can be brought down to only the cost of a semi-honest dot product computation (which is
3
ring elements and independent of the vector dimension), where the cost due to verification can be amortized away by setting
\mathsf{d},m
appropriately.

Given vectors
\vec{\mathbf{u}}=(\mathsf{u}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{u}_{\mathsf{d}}),\vec{\mathbf{v}}=(\mathsf{v}_{1},\ldots,\mathsf{v}_{\mathsf{d}})
, the semi-honest dot product protocol proceeds as follows. The parties, using the shared key setup, non-interactively generate
3-out-of-3
additive shares of zero using
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{zero}}
(§
4.1.1.1
), i.e
P_{i}
has
\zeta_{i}
, such that
\zeta_{1}+\zeta_{2}+\zeta_{3}=0
. Then, parties proceed with generating the
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of
\mathsf{w}=\vec{\mathbf{u}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{v}}
as:

\displaystyle P_{1}\penalty\ \text{ computes and sends }\penalty\ \mathsf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=\zeta_{1}+\sum_{j=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(\mathsf{u}_{j}^{1}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{3}+\mathsf{u}_{j}^{3}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{1}+\mathsf{u}_{j}^{3}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{3})\penalty\ \text{ to }\penalty\ P_{2}

\displaystyle P_{2}\penalty\ \text{ computes and sends }\penalty\ \mathsf{y}_{2}

\displaystyle=\zeta_{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(\mathsf{u}_{j}^{2}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{3}+\mathsf{u}_{j}^{3}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{2}+\mathsf{u}_{j}^{2}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{2})\penalty\ \text{ to }\penalty\ P_{3}

\displaystyle P_{3}\penalty\ \text{ computes and sends }\penalty\ \mathsf{y}_{3}

\displaystyle=\zeta_{3}+\sum_{j=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(\mathsf{u}_{j}^{1}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{2}+\mathsf{u}_{j}^{2}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{1}+\mathsf{u}_{j}^{1}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{1})\penalty\ \text{ to }\penalty\ P_{1}

(8.1)

Now, to complete the
\langle\cdot\rangle
-sharing of
\mathsf{w}
, parties locally set
\mathsf{w}^{1}=\mathsf{y}_{3}
,
\mathsf{w}^{2}=\mathsf{y}_{2}
and
\mathsf{w}^{3}=\mathsf{y}_{1}
. To check the correctness of the computation
\langle\mathsf{w}\rangle=\langle\vec{\mathbf{u}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{v}}\rangle
, each
P_{i}\in\mathcal{P}
needs to prove that the
\mathsf{y}_{i}
it sent in the semi-honest protocol satisfies
8.1.1
. Without loss of generality, consider the case when
P_{i}=P_{1}
. Then, it has to prove

\displaystyle\zeta_{1}+\sum_{j=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(\mathsf{u}_{j}^{1}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{3}+\mathsf{u}_{j}^{3}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{1}+\mathsf{u}_{j}^{3}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{3})-\mathsf{y}_{1}=0

(8.2)

This difference in the expected message that should be sent (computed using
P_{1}
’s correct input shares) and the actual message sent by
P_{1}
is captured by a circuit
c
, defined below.

\displaystyle c\left(\{\mathsf{u}_{j}^{1},\mathsf{u}_{j}^{3},\mathsf{v}_{j}^{1},\mathsf{v}_{j}^{3}\}_{j=1}^{\mathsf{d}},\zeta_{1},\mathsf{w}_{1}\right)=\zeta_{1}+\sum_{j=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(\mathsf{u}_{j}^{1}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{3}+\mathsf{u}_{j}^{3}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{1}+\mathsf{u}_{j}^{3}\mathsf{v}_{j}^{3})-\mathsf{y}_{1}

(8.3)

Here,
c
takes as input
u=4\mathsf{d}+2
values:
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of
\vec{\mathbf{u}},\vec{\mathbf{v}}
held by
P_{1}
, i.e.
\{\mathsf{u}_{j}^{1},\mathsf{u}_{j}^{3},\mathsf{v}_{j}^{1},\mathsf{v}_{j}^{3}\}_{j=1}^{\mathsf{d}}
, the additive share of zero,
\zeta_{1}
, that
P_{1}
holds, and the additive share
\mathsf{y}_{1}
sent by
P_{1}
. For correct computation with respect to
P_{1}
, we require the difference in the expected message and the actual message to be
0
, i.e.,

\displaystyle c\left(\{\mathsf{u}_{j}^{1},\mathsf{u}_{j}^{3},\mathsf{v}_{j}^{1},\mathsf{v}_{j}^{3}\}_{j=1}^{\mathsf{d}},\zeta_{1},\mathsf{w}_{1}\right)=0

(8.4)

We now explain how to verify the correctness for
m
dot product tuples assuming that the operations are carried out over a prime-order field. The verification can be extended to support operations over rings following the techniques of
[
24
,
27
]
.
To verify the correctness for
m
dot product tuples,
\{\vec{\mathbf{u}}_{k},\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{k},\mathsf{w}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{m}
where
\mathsf{w}_{k}=\vec{\mathbf{u}}_{k}\odot\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{k}
, the output of
c
(which is the difference in the expected and actual message sent) for each of the corresponding dot product tuple must be
0
.
To check correctness of all dot products
at once
, it suffices to check if a random linear combination of the output of each
c
(for each dot product) is
0
. This is because the random linear combination of the differences will be
0
with high probability if
\mathsf{w}_{k}=\vec{\mathbf{u}}_{k}\odot\vec{\mathbf{v}}_{k}
for each
k\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
.
We remark that the definition of
c(\cdot)
in
[
27
]
enables the verification of only multiplication triples. With the re-definition of
c
as in
8.3
, we can now verify the correctness of dot products while the rest of the verification steps remain similar to that in
[
27
]
. We elaborate on the details next.

A verification circuit, constructed as follows, enables
P_{i}
to prove the correctness of the additive share of
\mathsf{w}
that it sent, for
m
instances of dot product at once. Note that the proof system is designed for the distributed-verifier setting where the proof generated by
P_{i}
will be shared among
P_{i-1},P_{i+1}
, who can together verify its correctness. First, a sub-circuit
g
is defined as follows: group
L
small
c
circuits and take a random linear combination of the values on their output wires. Since each
c
circuit takes
u=4\mathsf{d}+2
inputs as described earlier,
g
takes in
uL
inputs. Precisely,
g
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle g(x_{1},\ldots,x_{uL})=\sum_{k=1}^{L}\theta_{k}\cdot c(x_{(k-1)u+1},\ldots,x_{(k-1)u+u})

Since there are total
m
dot products to be verified, there will be
M=m/L
sub-circuits
g
. Looking ahead, this grouping technique enables obtaining a sub-linear communication cost for verification because the communication cost turns out to be
\mathcal{O}(uL+M)
and setting
uL=M
gives the desired result. The sub-circuits
g
make up the circuit
G
which outputs a random linear combination of the values on the output wires of each
g
, i.e:

\displaystyle G(x_{1},\ldots,x_{um})=\sum_{k=1}^{M}\eta_{k}\cdot g(x_{(k-1)uL+1},\ldots,x_{(k-1)uL+uL})

Here,
\theta_{k}
and
\eta_{k}
are randomly sampled (non-interactively) by all parties.
To prove correctness,
P_{i}
needs to prove that
G
outputs
0
. For this,
P_{i}
defines
f_{1}\ldots,f_{uL}
random polynomials of degree
M
, one for each input wire of
g
. For
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
and
j\in\{1,\ldots,uL\}
,
f_{j}(0)
is chosen randomly and
f_{j}(\ell)=x_{(\ell-1)u+j}
(i.e the
j\text{th}
input of the
\ell\text{th}

g
gate).

P_{i}
further defines a
2M
degree polynomial
p(\cdot)
on the output wires of
g
, i.e
p(\cdot)=g(f_{1},\ldots,f_{uL})
where
p(\ell)
for
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,M\}
is the output of the
\ell
th
g
gate. The additional
M+1
points required to interpolate the
2M
degree polynomial
p
, are obtained by evaluating
f_{1},\ldots,f_{uL}
on
M+1
additional points, followed by an application of
g
circuit. The proof generated by
P_{i}
consists of
f_{1}(0),\ldots,f_{uL}(0)
and the coefficients of
p
. Recall that since we are in the distributed-verifier setting, the prover
P_{i}
additively shares the proof with
P_{i-1},P_{i+1}
. Note here, that shares of
f_{1}(0),\ldots,f_{uL}(0)
can be generated non-interactively.

To verify the proof, verifiers
P_{i-1},P_{i+1}
need to check if the output of
G
is
0
. This can be verified by computing the output of
G
as
b=\sum_{\ell=1}^{M}\eta_{\ell}\cdot p(\ell)
and checking if
b=0
, where
\eta_{\ell}
’s are non-interactively sampled by all after the proof is sent. If
p
is defined correctly, then this is indeed a random linear combination of the outputs of all the
g
-circuits. This necessitates the second check to verify the correctness of
p
as per its definition i.e
p(\cdot)=g(f_{1}(\cdot),\ldots,f_{uL}(\cdot))
. This is performed by checking if
p(r)=g(f_{1}(r),\ldots,f_{uL}(r))
for a random
r\notin\{1,\ldots,M\}
(for privacy to hold) sampled non-interactively by all after the proof is sent.
For the first check, verifiers can locally compute additive shares of
b
(using the additive shares of coefficients of
p
obtained as part of the proof) and reconstruct
b
to check for equality with
0
.
For the second, verifiers locally compute additive shares of
p(r)
using the shares of coefficients of
p
, and shares of
f_{1}(r),\ldots,f_{uL}(r)
by interpolating
f_{1},\ldots,f_{uL}
using (
P_{i}
’s) inputs to the
c
-circuits which are implicitly additively shared between them (owing to the replicated sharing property). Verifiers exchange these values among themselves, reconstruct it and check if
p(r)=g(f_{1}(r),\ldots,f_{uL}(r))
.
Note that the messages computed and exchanged by the verifiers depend only on the proof sent by
P_{i}
and the random values (
r,\eta
) sampled by all.
P_{i}
can independently compute these messages. Thus, to prevent a verifier from falsely rejecting a correct proof, we use
\mathsf{jsnd}
to exchange these messages.
To optimize the communication cost further, it suffices if a single verifier computes the output of verification.

Setting the parameters:

The proof sent by
P_{i}
consists of the constant terms
f_{j}(0)
for
j\in\{1,\ldots,uL\}
and
2M+1
coefficients of
p
. The former can be can be generated non-interactively. Hence,
P_{i}
needs to communicate
2M+1
elements to the verifiers (one of which can be performed non-interactively). The message sent by the verifier consists of the additive share of
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M}\eta_{\ell}\cdot p(\ell)
(for the first check) and
f_{1}(r),\ldots,f_{uL}(r),p(r)
(for the second check). Thus, the verifier communicates
uL+2
elements. As the proof is executed three times, each time with one party acting as the prover and the other two acting as the verifiers, overall, each party communicates
uL+2M+3
elements. Setting
uL=2M
and
M=\frac{m}{L}
results in the total communication required for verifying
m
dot products to be
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\mathsf{d}m})
. Thus, verifying a single dot product has an amortized cost of
\mathcal{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\mathsf{d}}{m}}\right)
which can be made very small by appropriately setting the values of
\mathsf{d},m
. Thus, the (amortized) cost of a maliciously secure dot product protocol can be made equal to that of a semi-honest dot product protocol, which is
3
ring elements.

To support verification over rings
[
27
]
, verification operations are carried out on the extended ring
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}/f(x)
, which is the ring of all polynomials with coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
modulo a polynomial
f
, of degree
d
, irreducible over
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. Each element in
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
is lifted to a
d
-degree polynomial in
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}[x]/f(x)
(which results in blowing up the communication by a factor
d
). Thus, the per party communication amounts to
(uL+2M+3)d
elements of
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
for verifying
m
dot products of vector size
\mathsf{d}
where
u=4\mathsf{d}+2
. Further, the probability of a cheating prover is bounded by
\frac{2^{(\ell-1)d}\cdot 2M+1}{2^{\ell d}-M}
(cf. Theorem 4.7 of
[
27
]
).
Thus, if
\gamma
is such that
2^{\gamma}\geq 2M
, then the cheating probability is

\displaystyle\frac{2^{(\ell-1)d}\cdot 2M+1}{2^{\ell d}-M}\leq\frac{2^{(\ell-1)d}\cdot 2^{\gamma}+1}{2^{\ell d}-M}\approx 2^{-(d-\gamma)}

We note that both,
[
27
]
and our technique require a communication cost of
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{m\mathsf{d}})
ring elements for verifying
m
dot products of vector size
\mathsf{d}
. This is because multiplication is a special case of dot product with
\mathsf{d}=1
. However, since our verification is for dot products, we can get away with performing only
m
semi-honest dot products whose cost is equivalent to computing
m
semi-honest multiplications, whereas
[
27
]
requires to execute
m\mathsf{d}
multiplications (as their technique can only verify correctness of multiplications), resulting in a dot product cost dependent on the vector size.
Concretely, to get
40
bits of statistical security and for a vector size of
2^{10}
(CIFAR-10
[
88
]
dataset), the parameters mentioned above can be set as given in Table
8.1
.

Table 8.1
:
Cost of verification in terms of the number of ring elements communicated per dot product, and parameters for vector size
\mathsf{d}=2^{10}
and
40
bits of statistical security.
†
†
nicematrix-placeholder:
NiceTabular (nicematrix)

It is possible to further bring down the communication cost required for verifying
m
dot product tuples to
\mathcal{O}(\log(\mathsf{d}m))
at the expense of requiring more rounds by further extending the technique of
[
24
]
, which we leave as an exercise.
We refer readers to
[
27
]
for formal details.

Lemma 8.1.1

(Communication)
.

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp}}
(Fig.
8.1
) (in
SWIFT
) requires
3\ell
bits of communication in preprocessing, and
1
round and
3\ell
bits of communication in the online phase.

8.1.2
Bit Extraction

To compute most significant bit (
\mathsf{msb}
) of the value
\mathsf{v}
, note that
\mathsf{v}=\mathsf{v}_{1}+\mathsf{v}_{2}+\mathsf{v}_{3}
for
\mathsf{v}_{1}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}
,
\mathsf{v}_{2}=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}
and
\mathsf{v}_{3}=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}
as per the sharing semantics (cf. Table
4.2
). Parties generate the boolean sharing of
\mathsf{v}_{1},\mathsf{v}_{2},\mathsf{v}_{3}
using joint sharing protocol.
It has been shown in ABY3
[
101
]
that
\mathsf{v}=2c+s
where
FA
(\mathsf{v}_{1}[i],\mathsf{v}_{2}[i],\mathsf{v}_{3}[i])\rightarrow(c[i],s[i])
for
i\in\{0,\ldots,\ell-1\}
. Here
FA
denotes a Full Adder circuit while
s
and
c
denote the sum and carry bits respectively.
To summarize, parties execute
\ell
instances of
FA
in parallel to compute
{\llbracket c\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
and
{\llbracket s\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
. The
FA
’s are executed independently and require one round of communication. The final result is then computed as
\mathsf{msb}(2{\llbracket c\rrbracket}^{\bf B}+{\llbracket s\rrbracket}^{\bf B})
by evaluating the bit extraction circuit
[
101
,
113
]
.

8.1.3
Bit to Arithmetic

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}({\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket}^{\bf B})
(Fig.
8.3
) enables computing
\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
of a bit
\mathsf{b}
given its boolean sharing
{\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
. Let
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}
denotes the value of
\mathsf{b}\in\{0,1\}
over the arithmetic ring
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
. Using our sharing semantics,

\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}})^{\sf R}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}(1-2\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R})

(8.5)

Let
\mathsf{u}=\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}
and
\mathsf{v}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}
.

Input(s):

{\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
,
Output:

\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}\rrbracket
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

1.

(P_{1},P_{3})
,

and

.

.

4.

Compute the
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of
\sigma_{1}^{\sf R}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3})^{\sf R}
using
\Pi_{\mathsf{MultPre}}
.

5.

Locally compute
\langle\mathsf{u}\rangle=\langle\sigma\rangle+\langle(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3})^{\sf R}\rangle-2\langle\sigma(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3})^{\sf R}\rangle
.

\justify

Online:

Let
\mathsf{y}=\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}
.

1.

Locally compute the following:

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{1}=\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{u}^{1}(1-2\mathsf{v})\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{2},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{2}=\mathsf{u}^{2}(1-2\mathsf{v})\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{3}=\mathsf{u}^{3}(1-2\mathsf{v})

2.

(P_{1},P_{3}),(P_{2},P_{3}),(P_{1},P_{2})
execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
on
\mathsf{y}_{1},\mathsf{y}_{2},\mathsf{y}_{3}
to generate the respective
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares.

3.

Compute
\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{y}_{1}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{y}_{2}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{y}_{3}\rrbracket
.

Figure 8.3
:

.

During preprocessing, parties locally generate
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of

,

similar to

\displaystyle(\mathsf{b}_{1}\mathsf{b}_{2})^{\sf R}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}+(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})^{\sf R}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}

\displaystyle\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}\mathsf{v}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}})^{\sf R}(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{})=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}}-\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}}

8.1.4.1
Sum of Bit Injections

Given
m
pair of values in the shared form,
\{{\llbracket\mathsf{b}_{i}\rrbracket}^{\bf B},\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{i}\rrbracket\}_{i\in[m]}
, the goal of
\Pi_{\mathsf{bitInjS}}
is to compute the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-share of
\mathsf{z}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathsf{b}_{i}^{\sf R}\cdot\mathsf{v_{i}}
. For this, parties execute the preprocessing corresponding to
m
bit injections of the form
\mathsf{b}_{i}^{\sf R}\cdot\mathsf{v_{i}}
.

In the online phase, parties locally compute an additive sharing of
\mathsf{z}_{i}
, corresponding to
\mathsf{b}_{i}^{\sf R}\cdot\mathsf{v_{i}}
first. Instead of generating the
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing for each of the
m
terms, parties locally add the shares and execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
on the result. This results in an online communication independent of
m
.

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{})

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ -\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}-\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}-\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}-(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})^{\sf R}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}{}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}

8.1.5
Truncation Pair Generation (
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
)

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{trgen}}
(Fig.
8.4
) allows parties to generate a truncation pair of the form
(\langle\mathsf{r}\rangle,\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket)
for a random
\mathsf{r}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
. Analogous to the approach of ABY3
[
101
]
, parties non-interactively generate the boolean sharing of an
\ell
-bit value
\mathsf{r}
first. Parties then discard the shares for the lower
x
bit positions to obtain the boolean shares of the truncated value denoted by
\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}
. To obtain the arithmetic shares of the truncation pair, we do not rely on the approach of ABY3 as it requires more rounds. Instead, we implicitly perform a
boolean to arithmetic
conversion using techniques from bit to arithmetic protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
.

\justify

Let
i\in\{0,\ldots,\ell-1\}
and
j\in\{0,\ldots,\ell-1-x\}
. Here
x
denotes the precision in FPA semantics.

1.

P_{s},P_{3}
for
s\in\{1,2\}
sample
\ell
-bits, denoted by
\mathsf{r}_{s}[i]
.

2.

Define
\ell
-bit value
\mathsf{r}=\mathsf{r}_{1}\oplus\mathsf{r}_{2}
. i.e.
\mathsf{r}[i]=\mathsf{r}_{1}[i]\oplus\mathsf{r}_{2}[i]
.

3.

P_{s},P_{3}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
on
(\mathsf{r}_{s}[i])^{\sf R}
to generate the respective
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares.

4.

Locally compute
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of
(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}
and
(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}
1
1

1

by discarding the
\mathsf{m}
value that is set to
0
as per Table
4.3
.

5.

Define
\ell
-sized vectors
\vec{\mathbf{a}},\vec{\mathbf{b}}
as:
\mathsf{a}_{j}=2^{i+1}(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}
and
\mathsf{b}_{i}=(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}
.

6.

Define
(\ell-x)
-sized vectors
\vec{\mathbf{c}},\vec{\mathbf{d}}
as:
\mathsf{c}_{j}=2^{j+1}(\mathsf{r}_{1}[j+x])^{\sf R}
and
\mathsf{b}_{j}=(\mathsf{r}_{2}[j+x])^{\sf R}
.

7.

Locally compute
\langle\vec{\mathbf{a}}\rangle,\langle\vec{\mathbf{b}}\rangle,\langle\vec{\mathbf{c}}\rangle,\langle\vec{\mathbf{d}}\rangle
.

8.

Compute the
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of
\mathsf{x}=\vec{\mathbf{a}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{b}}
and
\mathsf{y}=\vec{\mathbf{c}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{d}}
using
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotpPre}}
protocol
2
2

2

\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket
can be computed by locally setting
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}}=0
.

9.

Locally compute
\langle\mathsf{r}\rangle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}(\langle(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}\rangle+\langle(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}\rangle)-\langle\mathsf{x}\rangle
.

10.

Locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}\rrbracket=\sum_{j=0}^{\ell-1-x}2^{j}(\llbracket(\mathsf{r}_{1}[j+x])^{\sf R}\rrbracket+\llbracket(\mathsf{r}_{2}[j+x])^{\sf R}\rrbracket)-\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket
.

Figure 8.4
:
Truncation pair generation in
SWIFT
.

Concretely,
P_{1},P_{3}
sample an
\ell
-bit value
\mathsf{r}_{1}
while
P_{2},P_{3}
sample
\mathsf{r}_{2}
. For the
i^{th}
bit position, define
\mathsf{r}[i]=\mathsf{r}_{1}[i]\oplus\mathsf{r}_{2}[i]
for
i\in\{0,\ldots,\ell-1\}
. For
\mathsf{r}
defined as above, we have
\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}[j]=\mathsf{r}_{1}[j+x]\oplus\mathsf{r}_{2}[j+x]
for

. Further,

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\mathsf{r}[i]=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\left(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i]\oplus\mathsf{r}_{2}[i]\right)=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\left((\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}+(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}-2(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}\cdot(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\left((\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}+(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}\right)-\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\left(\left(2^{i+1}(\mathsf{r}_{1}[i])^{\sf R}\right)\cdot(\mathsf{r}_{2}[i])^{\sf R}\right)

(8.10)

Similarly, for
\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}=\sum_{j=0}^{\ell-1-x}2^{j}\left((\mathsf{r}_{1}[j+x])^{\sf R}+(\mathsf{r}_{2}[j+x])^{\sf R}\right)-\sum_{j=0}^{\ell-1-x}\left(\left(2^{j+1}(\mathsf{r}_{1}[j+x])^{\sf R}\right)\cdot(\mathsf{r}_{2}[j+x])^{\sf R}\right)

(8.11)

Given the boolean shares, parties can evaluate (
8.1.5
) and (
8.11
) using two instances of

as shown in Fig.
8.4
.

(Communication)

8.1.6
Equality Test (
\Pi_{\mathsf{eq}}
)

To check whether
\mathsf{a}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{=}}\mathsf{b}
or not, given
\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
,
\Pi_{\mathsf{eq}}
proceeds with parties locally computing
\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket-\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
. According to our sharing semantics,
\mathsf{y}
can be written as
\mathsf{y}=\mathsf{y}_{1}-\mathsf{y}_{2}
where
\mathsf{y}_{1}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{3}
and
\mathsf{y}_{2}=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{1}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{2}
.

During preprocessing,
(P_{1},P_{3})
and
(P_{2},P_{3})
generate the
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
-shares of
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{1}
and
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{2}
respectively using
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
. Parties then compute
{\llbracket\mathsf{y}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
using a boolean adder (PPA) circuit.
During the online phase,
P_{1},P_{2}
generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{y}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
using
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
. Note that
\mathsf{a}=\mathsf{b}
implies
\mathsf{y}_{1}=\mathsf{y}_{2}
and hence all the bits of
\mathsf{v}=\overline{(\mathsf{y}_{1}\oplus\mathsf{y}_{2})}
should be
1
. As mentioned in the introduction of Part II (
II
), parties use four input AND gates and a tree structure, where
4
bits are taken at a time and the AND of them is computed in one go.

8.2
Mixed Protocol Framework

Table
8.2
compares our sharing conversions with ABY3
[
101
]
. For uniformity, we consider a function,
F
, to be computed on an
\ell
-bit inputs
\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}
using a garbled circuit (GC) in the mixed framework, which gives an
\ell
-bit output
\mathsf{z}=\mathsf{F(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})}
, where
\ell
denotes the ring size in bits. Let
\mathsf{G}^{\sf F}
denote the corresponding GC. In the table,
\mathsf{G}^{\sf Sn}
denotes a
n
-input garbled subtraction circuit;
\mathsf{G}^{\sf An}
denotes
n
-input garbled addition circuit;
\hat{\mathsf{G}}
denotes the garbled circuit with decoding information;
\mathsf{G}^{\sf n_{1}\times 1,\ldots,n_{m}\times m}
denotes
{\sf n_{i}}
instances of GC
\mathsf{G}^{\sf i}
for
i\in\{1,\dots,{\sf m}\}
and
|\mathsf{G}^{\sf n_{1}\times 1,\ldots,n_{m}\times m}|
denotes its size.

:

[

]
and

.

8.2.1
Conversions involving Garbled World

Assume the GC is required to compute a function
f
on inputs
\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
and let the output be
f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})
. All the conversions described are for the 2 GC variant. Conversions for the 1 GC variant are straightforward, hence we omit the details.

Case I: Boolean-Garbled-Boolean

Since the inputs to the GC are available in boolean form, say
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf B},{\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
, parties generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{x}\rrbracket}^{\bf C},{\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket}^{\bf C}
by invoking the garbled sharing protocol
\mathrm{\Pi}_{\mathsf{Sh}}^{\bf G}
.

(P_{1},P_{3})
sample
\mathsf{R}_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
to mask the function output,
f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})
, and generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{R}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
and
{\llbracket\mathsf{R}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
. Similarly,
(P_{2},P_{3})
sample
\mathsf{R}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
and generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{R}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
and
{\llbracket\mathsf{R}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
.
Garblers
P_{g}\in\{P_{1},P_{3}\}
garble the circuit which computes
\mathsf{z}=f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})\oplus\mathsf{R}_{1}\oplus\mathsf{R}_{2}
, and send the GC along with the decoding information to evaluator
P_{1}
. Analogous steps are performed for evaluator
P_{2}
. Upon GC evaluation and output decoding, evaluators obtain
\mathsf{z}=f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})\oplus\mathsf{R}_{1}\oplus\mathsf{R}_{2}
, and jointly boolean share
\mathsf{z}
to generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
. Parties then compute
{\llbracket f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})\rrbracket}^{\bf B}={\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}\oplus{\llbracket\mathsf{R}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}\oplus{\llbracket\mathsf{R}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
.

Case II: Boolean-Garbled-Arithmetic

This is similar to
Case I
except that the circuit which computes
\mathsf{z}=f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})+\mathsf{R}_{1}+\mathsf{R}_{2}
is garbled instead. Boolean sharing of
\mathsf{z}
is replaced with arithmetic, followed by computing
\llbracket f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket-\llbracket\mathsf{R}_{1}\rrbracket-\llbracket\mathsf{R}_{2}\rrbracket
.

Cases III & IV: Input in Arithmetic Sharing

The function to be computed
f(\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y})
, is modified as
f^{\prime}(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{2},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{3})=f(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{1}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{2}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{3})
where inputs
\mathsf{x},\mathsf{y}
are replaced by the sets
\{\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3}\}
,
\{\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{1},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{2},\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{3}\}
. The circuit to be garbled thus, corresponds to the function
f^{\prime}
. Parties generate
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf G}
-shares via
\mathrm{\Pi}_{\mathsf{Sh}}^{\bf G}
, following which, parties proceed with the rest of the computation whose steps are similar to
Case I
, and
II
, depending on the requirement on the output sharing. For the instance with
P_{1}
as the evaluator, function
f^{\prime}
can be further optimized as
f(\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{1}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{3},\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{y}}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{1}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{3})
with
\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{x}}}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{x}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{x}}^{2}
and
\mathsf{\alpha}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{y}}}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{y}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{y}}^{2}
. Similar optimization can be done for the other garbling instance as well.

8.2.2
Other Conversions

Arithmetic to Boolean

To convert arithmetic sharing of
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
to boolean, observe that
\mathsf{v}=\mathsf{v}_{1}+\mathsf{v}_{2}
where
\mathsf{v}_{1}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
and
\mathsf{v}_{2}=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
. Thus,
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
can be computed as
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}={\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}+{\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
. For this, parties generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
in the preprocessing, and
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
can be generated in the online locally by setting
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}=\mathsf{v}_{1}
and
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}_{2}}=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}_{3}}=0
. The protocol appears in Fig.
8.5
. Boolean addition, when instantiated using the adder of ABY2.0
[
113
]
, requires
\log_{4}(\ell)
rounds.

Let
\mathsf{v}_{1}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}
and
\mathsf{v}_{2}=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

1.

Non-interactively generate
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
-shares of
\mathsf{u}_{i}=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{i}
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
using
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
( §
4.2.1.1
).

2.

Evaluate
FA
(\mathsf{v}_{1}[i],\mathsf{v}_{2}[i],\mathsf{v}_{3}[i])\rightarrow(c[i],s[i])
for
i\in\{0,\ldots,\ell-1\}
to generate
{\llbracket c[i]\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
and
{\llbracket s[i]\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
.

3.

Compute
2{\llbracket c\rrbracket}^{\bf B}+{\llbracket s\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
using a boolean adder circuit
[
101
,
113
]
.

\justify

Online:

1.

Locally generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
as
\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}=\mathsf{v}_{1}
and
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}_{1}}^{=}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}_{2}}^{=}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}_{3}}^{=}0
.

2.

Compute
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}={\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}+{\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
using a boolean adder circuit
[
113
]
.

:

SWIFT
.

To generate
{\llbracket\mathsf{v}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
, let
\mathsf{v}_{2}=\mathsf{u}_{1}+\mathsf{u}_{2}+\mathsf{u}_{3}
where
\mathsf{u}_{i}=-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{i}
for
i\in\{1,2,3\}
. Parties non-interactively generate the
{\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
-shares of
\mathsf{u}_{1},\mathsf{u}_{2},\mathsf{u}_{3}
using joint sharing protocol ( §
4.2.1.1
). For a full adder circuit
FA
(\mathsf{v}_{1}[i],\mathsf{v}_{2}[i],\mathsf{v}_{3}[i])\rightarrow(c[i],s[i])
for
i\in\{0,\ldots,\ell-1\}
, it has been shown in ABY3
[

]
that
\mathsf{v}_{2}=2c+s
where
s
and
c
denote the sum and carry bits respectively. Parties execute
\ell
instances of
FA
in parallel to compute
{\llbracket c\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
and
{\llbracket s\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
. The
FA
’s are executed independently and require one round of communication. The final result is then computed as
2{\llbracket c\rrbracket}^{\bf B}+{\llbracket s\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
by evaluating a boolean adder circuit
[

,
113
]
.

Boolean to Arithmetic

To convert a boolean sharing of
\mathsf{v}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
into an arithmetic sharing, note that

\displaystyle\mathsf{v}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\mathsf{v}[i]=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}\oplus\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}[i]})=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\left(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}(1-2\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}^{\sf R})\right)

where
{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}}^{\sf R},\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}^{\sf R}
denote the arithmetic value of bits
{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}},{\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}}
over the ring
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.
For each bit
\mathsf{v}[i]
of
\mathsf{v}
, parties generate the
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of
{{\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}}}^{\sf R}
in the preprocessing, similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
(Fig.
8.3
). During the online phase, additive shares for each bit
\mathsf{v}[i]
are locally computed similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
. Parties then multiply the
i
th share with
2^{i}
and locally add up to obtain an additive sharing of
\mathsf{v}
. The rest of the steps are similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
, and the formal protocol appears in Fig.
8.6
.

Let
\mathsf{v}[i]
denote the
i
th bit of
\mathsf{v}
. Let
\mathsf{p}_{i}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}^{\sf R}
, and
\mathsf{q}_{i}=\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{v}[i]}
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

1.

For
i\in\{0,1,\ldots,\ell-1\}
, execute the preprocessing of
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
(Fig.
8.3
) for each bit
\mathsf{v}[i]
, to generate
\langle{\mathsf{q}_{i}}\rangle=(\mathsf{q}_{i}^{1},\mathsf{q}_{i}^{2},\mathsf{q}_{i}^{3})
.

\justify

Online:

Let
\mathsf{y}_{i}=(\mathsf{v}[i])^{\sf R}
and
\mathsf{y}
denotes the arithmetic equivalent of
\mathsf{v}
.

1.

Locally compute the following:

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}^{1}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\mathsf{y}_{i}^{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}(\mathsf{p}_{i}+\mathsf{q}_{i}^{1}(1-2\mathsf{p}_{i}))

\displaystyle P_{2},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}^{2}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\mathsf{y}_{i}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}(\mathsf{q}_{i}^{2}(1-2\mathsf{p}_{i}))

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{y}^{3}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}\mathsf{y}_{i}^{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}2^{i}(\mathsf{q}_{i}^{3}(1-2\mathsf{p}_{i}))

2.

(P_{1},P_{3}),(P_{2},P_{3}),(P_{1},P_{2})
execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
on
\mathsf{y}^{1},\mathsf{y}^{2},\mathsf{y}^{3}
to generate the respective
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares.

3.

Locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{y}^{1}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{y}^{2}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{y}^{3}\rrbracket
.

Chapter 9
Tetrad
: 4PC Fair and Robust Protocols

This chapter provides details for the Layer II blocks of our 2PC framework
Tetrad
. Details for the Layer I blocks are provided in chapter
5
.

9.1
Building Blocks

9.1.1
Dot Product (Scalar Product)

Given
\llbracket\vec{\mathbf{a}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\vec{\mathbf{b}}\rrbracket
with
|\vec{\mathbf{a}}|=|\vec{\mathbf{b}}|=\mathsf{d}
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{dotp}}
(Fig.
9.1
) computes
\llbracket\mathsf{z}\rrbracket
such that
\mathsf{z}=(\vec{\mathbf{a}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{b}})^{\mathsf{t}}
if truncation is enabled, else
\mathsf{z}=\vec{\mathbf{a}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{b}}
.
For this, we combine the partial products from the multiplication protocol across
\mathsf{d}
multiplications and communicate them in a single shot. This makes the communication cost of the dot product independent of the vector size. The protocols for robust setting follows similarly from
Tetrad-R
I
and
Tetrad-R
II
.

Let
\mathsf{isTr}
be a bit that denotes whether truncation is required (
\mathsf{isTr}=1
) or not (
\mathsf{isTr}=0
).

Input(s):

\llbracket\vec{\mathbf{a}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\vec{\mathbf{b}}\rrbracket
.

Output:

\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket
where
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=0
and
\mathsf{z}=\vec{\mathbf{a}}\odot\vec{\mathbf{b}}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}{\mathsf{a}}_{i}{\mathsf{b}}_{i}
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

1.

Locally compute the following:

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{1}:\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}^{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{1}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{3}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{3}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{1}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{3}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{3})

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{2}:\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{2}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{3}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{3}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{2}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{2}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{2})

\displaystyle P_{0},P_{3}:\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}^{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{1}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{2}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{2}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{1}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{1}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{1})

2.

P_{0},P_{3}
and
P_{j}
sample random
{\mathsf{u}}^{j}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
. Let
{\mathsf{u}^{1}}+\mathsf{u}^{2}=\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}^{3}-\mathsf{r}
for a random
\mathsf{r}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

3.

P_{0},P_{3}
compute
\mathsf{r}=\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}^{3}-{\mathsf{u}^{1}}-\mathsf{u}^{2}
and set
\mathsf{q}=\mathsf{r}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
, else set
\mathsf{q}=\mathsf{r}
.
P_{0},P_{3}
execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}(P_{0},P_{3},\mathsf{q})
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket
.

4.

P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
sample random
{\mathsf{s}}_{1},{\mathsf{s}}_{2}\in_{R}\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
and set
{\mathsf{s}}={\mathsf{s}}_{1}+{\mathsf{s}}_{2}
1
1

1

For the fair protocol, it is enough for
P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
to sample
{\mathsf{s}}
directly.
.

P_{0}
sends
\mathsf{w}=\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}^{1}+\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}^{2}+{\mathsf{s}}
to
P_{3}
.

\justify

Online:

Let
\mathsf{y}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})-\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}\mathsf{b}_{i}}
.

1.

Locally compute the following:

\displaystyle P_{1}:\mathsf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{1}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}})+\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}^{1}+{\mathsf{u}}^{1}

\displaystyle P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{2}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}})+\gamma_{\vec{\mathbf{a}}\vec{\mathbf{b}}}^{2}+{\mathsf{u}}^{2}

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{3}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}})

2.

P_{1}
sends
\mathsf{y}_{1}
to
P_{2}
, while
P_{2}
sends
\mathsf{y}_{2}
to
P_{1}
, and they locally compute
\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}=(\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{y}_{2}+\mathsf{y}_{3})+\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}\mathsf{b}_{i}}
.

3.

If
\mathsf{isTr}=1
,
P_{1},P_{2}
set
\mathsf{p}=(\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r})^{\mathsf{t}}
, else
\mathsf{p}=\mathsf{z}-\mathsf{r}
.
P_{1},P_{2}
execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}(P_{1},P_{2},\mathsf{p})
to generate
\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket
.

4.

Parties locally compute
\llbracket\mathsf{o}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket
. Here
\mathsf{o}=\mathsf{z}^{\mathsf{t}}
if
\mathsf{isTr}=1
and
\mathsf{z}
otherwise.

5.

Verification:

P_{3}
computes
\mathsf{v}=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathsf{d}}(-(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{1}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}}^{2})\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}-(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{1}+\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{i}}^{2})\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{a}_{i}})+{\mathsf{u}^{1}}+\mathsf{u}^{2}+\mathsf{w}
and sends
\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{v})
to
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
. Parties
P_{1},P_{2}

\mathtt{abort}
iff
\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{v})\neq\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{y}_{1}+\mathsf{y}_{2}+{\mathsf{s}})
.

:

.

9.1.2
Bit Extraction

To compute most significant bit (
\mathsf{msb}
) of the value
\mathsf{v}
, note that
\mathsf{v}=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3})+(-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2})
as per the sharing semantics (cf. Table
5.2
).
P_{0},P_{3}
execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}^{\bf B}
on
(-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2})
during the preprocessing, while
P_{0},P_{3}
execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}^{\bf B}
on
(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{v}}-\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3})
during the online phase to generate the respective boolean sharing. Parties finally compute the result by evaluating the bit extraction circuit
[
101
,
113
]
.

9.1.3
Bit to Arithmetic

Protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}({\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket}^{\bf B})
(Fig.
9.2
) enables computing
\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
of a bit
\mathsf{b}
given its boolean sharing
{\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
. Let
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}
denotes the value of
\mathsf{b}\in\{0,1\}
over the arithmetic ring
\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
. Then for
\mathsf{b}=\mathsf{b}_{1}\oplus\mathsf{b}_{2}
, note that
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}=(\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\sf R}-\mathsf{b}_{2}^{\sf R})^{2}
. Let
\mathsf{b}_{1}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{\mathsf{b}}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{3}
and
\mathsf{b}_{2}=\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{1}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{v}}^{2}
. To compute
\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
, a pair of parties can generate the arithmetic sharing corresponding to
\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\sf R}
and
\mathsf{b}_{2}^{\sf R}
by executing
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
.
\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket
can be computed by invoking
\Pi_{\mathsf{Mult}}
once with inputs
\mathsf{x}=\mathsf{y}=\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\sf R}-\mathsf{b}_{2}^{\sf R}
.

We obtain a communication-optimized variant by trading off computation in the preprocessing. For this, note that

\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}})^{\sf R}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}(1-2\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R})

(9.1)

Let
\mathsf{v}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}
and
\mathsf{u}=\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}
. During the preprocessing,
P_{0}
generates
\langle\cdot\rangle
-sharing of
\mathsf{u}
and a check is executed to verify the correctness. The online phase consists of each pair of parties
(P_{1},P_{3})
,
(P_{2},P_{3})
and
(P_{1},P_{2})
locally computing an additive sharing of
\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}
, generating the corresponding
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-sharing using
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
, and locally adding the shares to obtain
\llbracket\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}\rrbracket
.

For verifying the
\langle\cdot\rangle
-sharing of
\mathsf{u}
by
P_{0}
, we let
P_{3}
obtain the bit
(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}})
as well as its arithmetic equivalent
(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}})^{\sf R}
in clear. Here
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}
denotes a random bit known to
P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
.
P_{3}
checks if both the received values are equivalent and raise a complaint if they are inconsistent. To catch a corrupt
P_{0}
from sharing a wrong
\mathsf{u}
value, parties use the
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of
\mathsf{u}
to compute
(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}})^{\sf R}
. Moreover, the verification steps are designed in such a way that every value communicated can be locally computed by at least two parties. This enables to use
\mathsf{jsnd}
for communication and hence the desired security guarantee is achieved.

Let
\mathsf{u}=\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}}
and
\mathsf{v}=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}
.

Input(s):

{\llbracket\mathsf{b}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
,
Output:

\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}\rrbracket
.

\justify

Preprocessing:

1.

Generation of
\langle\mathsf{u}\rangle
:
P_{0},P_{3},P_{i}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
sample
\mathsf{u}^{i}
.
P_{0}
sends
\mathsf{u}^{3}=\mathsf{u}-\mathsf{u}^{1}-\mathsf{u}^{2}
to
P_{1},P_{2}
.

2.

P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
sample random
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}\in\{0,1\}
and
\mathsf{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2^{\ell}}
.

3.

P_{1},P_{2}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3}\oplus\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}
to
P_{3}
.
P_{3}
locally sets
\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}=(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{1}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{2})\oplus(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}^{3}\oplus\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}})
.

4.

Parties compute:
P_{1},P_{0}:\mathsf{w}_{1}=\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R}+(\mathsf{u}^{1}+\mathsf{u}^{3})(1-2\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R})+\mathsf{r},\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{2},P_{0}:\mathsf{w}_{2}=(\mathsf{u}^{2})(1-2\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{b}}^{\sf R})-\mathsf{r}
.

5.

P_{1},P_{0}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{w}_{1}
to
P_{3}
, while
P_{2},P_{0}

\mathsf{jsnd}

\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{w}_{2})
to
P_{3}
.

6.

P_{3}
sets
\mathsf{flag}=\mathtt{continue}
if
\mathsf{H}((\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}}\oplus\mathsf{r}_{b})^{\sf R}-\mathsf{w}_{1})=\mathsf{H}(\mathsf{w}_{2})
, else
\mathsf{flag}=\mathtt{abort}
.
P_{3}
sends
\mathsf{flag}
to
P_{0},P_{1},P_{2}
. Parties mutually exchange the flag and accept the value that forms the majority.

7.

For robust setting, if
\mathsf{flag}=\mathtt{abort}
, then
\mathsf{TTP}=P_{1}
(or
P_{2}
).

\justify

Online:

Let
\mathsf{y}=\mathsf{b}^{\sf R}
.

1.

Locally compute the following:

\displaystyle P_{1},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{1}=\mathsf{v}+\mathsf{u}^{1}(1-2\mathsf{v})\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{2},P_{3}:\mathsf{y}_{2}=\mathsf{u}^{2}(1-2\mathsf{v})\penalty\ \penalty\ \Big|\penalty\ \penalty\ P_{1},P_{2}:\mathsf{y}_{3}=\mathsf{u}^{3}(1-2\mathsf{v})

2.

(P_{1},P_{3}),(P_{2},P_{3}),(P_{1},P_{2})
execute
\Pi_{\mathsf{JSh}}
on
\mathsf{y}_{1},\mathsf{y}_{2},\mathsf{y}_{3}
to generate the respective
\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
-shares.

3.

Compute
\llbracket\mathsf{y}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{y}_{1}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{y}_{2}\rrbracket+\llbracket\mathsf{y}_{3}\rrbracket
.

Figure 9.2
:

Tetrad
.

(Communication)

) requires

bits of communication in preprocessing, and
1
round and
3\ell
bits of communication in the online phase.

Proof 9.1.4
.

During preprocessing, generation of
\langle\mathsf{u}\rangle
involves communication of
\ell
bits from
P_{0}
to each of
P_{1},P_{2}
. As part of verification, two instances of
\mathsf{jsnd}
are executed, one on
1
bit and other on
\ell
bits. The communication for hash gets amortized over multiple instances. The online phase involves three instances of joint sharing protocol resulting in
1
rounds and a communication of
3\ell
bits. The costs follow from Lemma
5.1.3
.

9.1.3.1
Bit to Arithmetic:II

Similar to
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
protocol, given the boolean sharings
{\llbracket\mathsf{b}_{1}\rrbracket}^{\bf B},{\llbracket\mathsf{b}_{2}\rrbracket}^{\bf B}
, protocol
\Pi_{\mathsf{dbit2A}}
computes the arithmetic sharing of
(\mathsf{b}_{1}\mathsf{b}_{2})^{\sf R}
. Let
\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}
,
\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}
denote the value
(1-2\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R})
,
(1-2\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R})
respectively. Using (
9.1
), we can write

\displaystyle(\mathsf{b}_{1}\mathsf{b}_{2})^{\sf R}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}})^{\sf R}(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\oplus\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})^{\sf R}=(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}})(\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}^{\sf R}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}+\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\mathsf{m}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}^{\sf R}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}+(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})^{\sf R}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\Delta_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}

(9.2)

During preprocessing, the
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of
\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}
and
\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}
are computed similar to that of
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
(Fig.
9.2
). Once the
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares are generated, parties invoke the
\Pi_{\mathsf{MultS}}
(Fig.
5.4
) on
\langle\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\rangle
and
\langle\mathsf{\lambda}^{\sf R}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}}\rangle
to generate the
\langle\cdot\rangle
-shares of
(\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{1}}\mathsf{\lambda}_{\mathsf{b}_{2}})^{\sf R}
. The online phase is similar to that of
\Pi_{\mathsf{bit2A}}
protocol.
